### Q1. JEE Main 2026 (08 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements:
Statement I: Due to increase in van der Waals forces, the order of boiling points is CH$_3$CH$_2$CH$_2$I > CH$_3$CH$_2$I > CH$_3$I.
Statement II: As para-dichlorobenzene is more symmetric, its melting point is higher than ortho-dichlorobenzene, however its boiling point is lower than ortho-dichlorobenzene.
In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(D)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I is true because as the size of the alkyl group increases, the molecular mass and surface area increase. This leads to an increase in the magnitude of van der Waals forces, which in turn increases the boiling point. Thus, the correct order of boiling points is $\text{CH}_3\text{CH}_2\text{CH}_2\text{I} > \text{CH}_3\text{CH}_2\text{I} > \text{CH}_3\text{I}$.

Statement II is true because para-dichlorobenzene is more symmetrical than ortho-dichlorobenzene, allowing it to pack more efficiently in the crystal lattice, which results in a higher melting point. However, ortho-dichlorobenzene has a higher dipole moment than para-dichlorobenzene (which has a dipole moment of zero). The stronger dipole-dipole interactions in ortho-dichlorobenzene lead to a higher boiling point compared to para-dichlorobenzene.

Both Statement I and Statement II are true.

Answer: Both Statement I and Statement II are true

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2026 (08 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

n-Butane on monochlorination under photochemical condition gives an optically active compound "P". "P" on further chlorination gives dichloro compounds. The number of dichloro compounds obtained (ignore stereoisomers) is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3$
- **(B)** $4$
- **(C)** $5$
- **(D)** $6$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The structure of n-butane is $CH_3-CH_2-CH_2-CH_3$.

Monochlorination of n-butane gives two structural isomers: 1-chlorobutane and 2-chlorobutane.

Among these, 2-chlorobutane ($CH_3-CH_2-C^{*}HCl-CH_3$) contains a chiral carbon atom and is optically active. Thus, the optically active compound "P" is 2-chlorobutane.

Further chlorination of 2-chlorobutane can take place at any of the four carbon atoms, yielding the following dichloro compounds (ignoring stereoisomers):

1. Chlorination at C-1: $CH_3-CH_2-CHCl-CH_2Cl$ (1,2-dichlorobutane)

2. Chlorination at C-2: $CH_3-CH_2-CCl_2-CH_3$ (2,2-dichlorobutane)

3. Chlorination at C-3: $CH_3-CHCl-CHCl-CH_3$ (2,3-dichlorobutane)

4. Chlorination at C-4: $CH_2Cl-CH_2-CHCl-CH_3$ (1,3-dichlorobutane)

Therefore, 4 structurally distinct dichloro compounds are obtained.

Answer: $4$

</details>

### Q3. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Benzyl isocyanide can be obtained from
<img style="width: 300px" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/05APRS1__q66_d1.png" />
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A and B Only
- **(B)** A and C Only
- **(C)** B and D Only
- **(D)** D and E Only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) A and B Only**

**Detailed Solution:**

Reaction A: $C_6H_5CH_2Br + AgCN \rightarrow C_6H_5CH_2NC + AgBr$
$AgCN$ is predominantly covalent, so the nucleophilic attack occurs through the nitrogen atom, forming benzyl isocyanide.

Reaction B: $C_6H_5CH_2NH_2 + CHCl_3 + 3NaOH \rightarrow C_6H_5CH_2NC + 3NaCl + 3H_2O$
This is the carbylamine reaction. Primary amines react with chloroform and alkali to form isocyanides. Benzyl amine gives benzyl isocyanide.

Reaction C: $C_6H_5Br + AgCN \rightarrow$ No reaction
Aryl halides are generally unreactive towards nucleophilic substitution under normal conditions due to the partial double bond character of the C-Br bond.

Reaction D: $C_6H_5NH_2 + CHCl_3 + 3NaOH \rightarrow C_6H_5NC + 3NaCl + 3H_2O$
Aniline undergoes the carbylamine reaction to form phenyl isocyanide, not benzyl isocyanide.

Reaction E: $C_6H_5CH_2CH_2Br + KCN \rightarrow C_6H_5CH_2CH_2CN + KBr$
$KCN$ is predominantly ionic, so the nucleophilic attack occurs through the carbon atom, forming a cyanide (nitrile) rather than an isocyanide.

Thus, benzyl isocyanide is obtained from reactions A and B only.

Answer: A and B Only

</details>

### Q4. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Match the LIST-I with LIST-II
<table style="border: 1px solid black; border-collapse: collapse; width: auto;"><tr><th style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">List-I (Reaction)</th><th style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">List-II (Mechanism)</th></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">A. Williamson Synthesis</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">I. Electrophilic addition</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">B. Friedel Craft Reaction</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">II. Free radical substitution</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">C. Bromination of vinyl benzene</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">III. Nucleophilic substitution</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">D. Chlorination of toluene in light</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">IV. Electrophilic substitution</td></tr></table>
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-III, B-I, C-II, D-IV
- **(B)** A-III, B-IV, C-II, D-I
- **(C)** A-III, B-IV, C-I, D-II
- **(D)** A-I, B-III, C-IV, D-II

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) A-III, B-IV, C-I, D-II**

**Detailed Solution:**

Williamson synthesis involves the reaction of an alkyl halide with a sodium alkoxide to form an ether, which proceeds via a nucleophilic substitution ($S_N2$) mechanism. Thus, A matches with III.

Friedel-Crafts reaction involves the alkylation or acylation of an aromatic ring in the presence of a Lewis acid catalyst, which proceeds via an electrophilic substitution mechanism. Thus, B matches with IV.

Bromination of vinyl benzene (styrene) involves the addition of bromine across the carbon-carbon double bond, which proceeds via an electrophilic addition mechanism. Thus, C matches with I.

Chlorination of toluene in the presence of sunlight (or UV light) involves the substitution of hydrogen atoms in the methyl group by chlorine, which proceeds via a free radical substitution mechanism. Thus, D matches with II.

The correct matching is A-III, B-IV, C-I, D-II.

Answer: A-III, B-IV, C-I, D-II

</details>

### Q5. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Match the LIST-I with LIST-II
<table style="border: 1px solid black; border-collapse: collapse; width: auto;"><tr><th style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">List-I
Name of reaction</th><th style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">List-II
Reagent or catalyst used</th></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">A. Finkelstein reaction</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">I. $SbF_3$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">B. Swarts reaction</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">II. $Na$, dry ether</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">C. Sandmeyer's reaction</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">III. $NaI$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">D. Fittig reaction</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">IV. $Cu_2Cl_2$</td></tr></table>
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-I, B-IV, C-III, D-II
- **(B)** A-III, B-I, C-IV, D-II
- **(C)** A-IV, B-II, C-I, D-III
- **(D)** A-I, B-III, C-II, D-IV

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) A-III, B-I, C-IV, D-II**

**Detailed Solution:**

Finkelstein reaction involves the conversion of alkyl chlorides or bromides to alkyl iodides using $NaI$ in dry acetone. Thus, A matches with III.

Swarts reaction is used for the synthesis of alkyl fluorides by heating an alkyl chloride or bromide in the presence of a metallic fluoride such as $SbF_3$, $AgF$, $Hg_2F_2$, or $CoF_2$. Thus, B matches with I.

Sandmeyer's reaction involves the conversion of aryl diazonium salts to aryl halides using cuprous halides like $Cu_2Cl_2$ or $Cu_2Br_2$. Thus, C matches with IV.

Fittig reaction involves the coupling of two aryl halide molecules in the presence of sodium ($Na$) and dry ether to form a diaryl compound. Thus, D matches with II.

The correct matching is A-III, B-I, C-IV, D-II.

Answer: A-III, B-I, C-IV, D-II

</details>

### Q6. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Correct statements regarding alkyl halides $(R - X)$ among the following are:
A. Alcohol being less polar solvent as compared to water, alcoholic KOH favours elimination reaction with $R - X$.
B. Order of reactivity towards $S_N^1$ mechanism is $C_6 H_5 - CH_2 - Cl > C_6 H_5 - CHCl - C_6 H_5$.
C. Non substituted aryl halides exhibit properties similar to alkyl halides.
D. Vinyl chloride is an example of haloalkene and allyl chloride is an example of haloalkyne.
E. $R - Cl$ can be prepared by reacting $R - OH$ with $SOCl_2$ but $Ar - Cl$ cannot be prepared by reacting $Ar - OH$ with $SOCl_2$.
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A, B and C Only
- **(B)** B and D Only
- **(C)** A and E Only
- **(D)** D and E Only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) A and E Only**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement A is correct. Alcoholic KOH provides alkoxide ions which are stronger bases than hydroxide ions. Furthermore, alcohol is a less polar solvent than water, which decreases the solvation of the base, making it more effective for elimination reactions over substitution.

Statement B is incorrect. The reactivity towards the $S_N^1$ mechanism depends on the stability of the intermediate carbocation. The carbocation $C_6H_5-\overset{+}{C}H-C_6H_5$ is more stable than $C_6H_5-\overset{+}{C}H_2$ due to extended resonance stabilization from two phenyl rings. Thus, $C_6H_5-CHCl-C_6H_5$ is more reactive than $C_6H_5-CH_2-Cl$.

Statement C is incorrect. Aryl halides are much less reactive towards nucleophilic substitution than alkyl halides due to the partial double bond character of the C-X bond arising from resonance, as well as the $sp^2$ hybridization of the carbon atom.

Statement D is incorrect. Allyl chloride ($CH_2=CH-CH_2-Cl$) contains a carbon-carbon double bond, making it a haloalkene, not a haloalkyne.

Statement E is correct. Alkyl chlorides ($R-Cl$) can be easily prepared from alcohols ($R-OH$) using $SOCl_2$. However, phenols ($Ar-OH$) do not react with $SOCl_2$ to give aryl chlorides because the C-O bond in phenol has a partial double bond character due to resonance, making it difficult to cleave.

Therefore, only statements A and E are correct.

Answer: A and E Only

</details>

### Q7. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements :
<b>Statement (I) :</b> Benzyl chloride reacts faster in $S_N1$ mechanism than ethyl chloride.
<b>Statement (II) :</b> Ethyl carbocation intermediate is less stabilized by hyperconjugation than benzyl carbocation by resonance.
In the light of the above statements, choose the <b>correct answer</b> from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(D)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

The rate of $S_N1$ reaction depends on the stability of the carbocation intermediate formed during the rate-determining step.

Benzyl chloride undergoes ionization to form a benzyl carbocation ($C_6H_5CH_2^+$), whereas ethyl chloride forms an ethyl carbocation ($CH_3CH_2^+$). The benzyl carbocation is highly stabilized by the delocalization of the positive charge over the benzene ring through resonance. On the other hand, the ethyl carbocation is stabilized only by hyperconjugation involving three $\alpha$-hydrogens.

Since resonance provides significantly greater stabilization than hyperconjugation, the benzyl carbocation is much more stable than the ethyl carbocation. Consequently, benzyl chloride reacts faster in the $S_N1$ mechanism than ethyl chloride. Thus, Statement I is true.

Statement II correctly explains that the ethyl carbocation intermediate is less stabilized by hyperconjugation compared to the resonance stabilization of the benzyl carbocation. Thus, Statement II is also true.

Both Statement I and Statement II are true.

Answer: Both Statement I and Statement II are true

</details>

### Q8. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Grignard reagent $\mathrm{RMgBr}(\mathrm{P})$ reacts with water and forms a gas (Q). One gram of Q occupies $1.4 \mathrm{dm}^{3}$ at STP. (P) on reaction with dry ice in dry ether followed by $\mathrm{H}_{3} \mathrm{O}^{+}$forms a compound (Z). 0.1 mole of (Z) will weigh $\_\_\_\_$ g. (Nearest integer)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

Grignard reagent RMgBr reacts with water to produce gas Q.
One gram of Q occupies 1.4 L at STP.
Moles of Q = 1.4/22.4 = 0.0625 mol, so molar mass = 1/0.0625 = 16 g/mol, identifying Q as CH₄.
Thus P = CH₃MgBr.
When P reacts with dry ice: $\text{CH}_3\text{MgBr} + \text{CO}_2 \rightarrow \text{CH}_3\text{COOMgBr}$.
Upon treatment with H₃O⁺, this gives Z = CH₃COOH (acetic acid).
Molar mass of Z = 60 g/mol.
Mass of 0.1 mol = 0.1 × 60 = 6 g.

</details>

### Q9. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Match the LIST-I with LIST-II 
<table><tr><th>List-I (Chloro derivative)</th><th>List-II (Example)</th></tr><tr><td>A. Vinyl Chloride</td><td>I. $CH_2 = CH - CH_2Cl$</td></tr><tr><td>B. Benzyl chloride</td><td>II. $CH_3 = CH(Cl)CH_3$</td></tr><tr><td>C. Alkyl chloride</td><td>III. $CH_2 = CHCl$</td></tr><tr><td>D. Allyl chloride</td><td>IV. <img style="width:150px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/24S1_q_60_1_v2.png"></td></tr></table>
 Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-III, B-IV, C-I, D-II
- **(B)** A-I, B-II, C-IV, D-III
- **(C)** A-IV, B-I, C-III, D-II
- **(D)** A-III, B-IV, C-II, D-I

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) A-III, B-IV, C-II, D-I**

**Detailed Solution:**

Vinyl Chloride has Cl directly attached to a C=C double bond, matching III: CH₂=CHCl. 
Benzyl Chloride has CH₂Cl attached to a benzene ring, matching IV (benzene with CH₂Cl substituent). 
Alkyl Chloride is a general chlorine-substituted alkane, matching II: CH₂-CH(Cl)CH₃ (a standard secondary alkyl chloride). 
Allyl Chloride has CH₂Cl attached to the allylic position (adjacent to C=C), matching I: CH₂=CH-CH₂Cl. 
The correct matching is A-III, B-IV, C-II, D-I.

</details>

### Q10. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements: 
 <b>Statement I</b>: ' $\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{Cl}^{\prime}$ bond is stronger in $\mathrm{CH}_{2}=\mathrm{CH}-\mathrm{Cl}$ than $\mathrm{CH}_{3}-\mathrm{CH}_{2}-\mathrm{Cl}$ 
 <b>Statement II</b>: The given optically active molecule,
<img style="width:200px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/24S1_q_54_1.png">
on hydrolysis gives a solution that can rotate the plane polarized light. 
 In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(C)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(D)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Statement I is true but Statement II is false**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I: In vinyl chloride (CH₂=CH-Cl), the C-Cl bond involves an sp² hybridized carbon, while in ethyl chloride (CH₃-CH₂-Cl), it involves an sp³ carbon. The sp² carbon has greater s-character, forming a shorter and stronger C-Cl bond. Additionally, resonance stabilization from the Cl atom's p-orbital overlapping with the π system further strengthens the vinyl C-Cl bond. Statement I is TRUE.

Statement II: The optically active molecule shown has a stereogenic center bearing Me, Ph, Et, and Cl groups. Upon hydrolysis via nucleophilic substitution (typically SN1 for tertiary or secondary centers), the C-Cl bond breaks to form an alcohol. SN1 mechanism generates a carbocation intermediate, allowing nucleophilic attack from both faces, producing a racemic mixture of the alcohol product. A racemic solution cannot rotate polarized light as the d- and l-enantiomers cancel each other's rotations. Statement II is FALSE.

</details>

### Q11. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider all the structural isomers with molecular formula $\mathrm{C}_{5} \mathrm{H}_{11} \mathrm{Br}$ are separately treated with $\mathrm{KOH}(\mathrm{aq})$ to give respective substitution products, without any rearrangement. The number of products which can exhibit optical isomerism from these is $\_\_\_\_$.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

For C₅H₁₁Br structural isomers to show optical isomerism after KOH substitution (Br→OH), the resulting product must have a chiral center (asymmetric carbon with four different groups). 
Possible carbon skeletons: n-pentane, 2-methylbutane, 2,2-dimethylpropane. 
Analysis of isomers: 
2-bromopentane → CH₃CHOHCH₂CH₂CH₃: C2 bonded to H, OH, CH₃, and CH₂CH₂CH₃ (chiral). 
3-bromopentane → CH₃CH₂CHOHCH₂CH₃: C3 bonded to H, OH, CH₂CH₃, CH₂CH₃ (NOT chiral). 
3-bromo-2-methylbutane → CH₃CH(CH₃)CHOHCH₃: Chiral center present. 
1-bromo-3-methylbutane → HOCH₂CH(CH₃)CH₂CH₃: C2 bonded to H, OH, CH₃, and CH₂CH₃ (chiral). 
2,2-dimethylpropane derivatives: No chiral centers possible. 
Compounds giving chiral products: 2-bromopentane, 3-bromo-2-methylbutane, and 1-bromo-3-methylbutane. Total = 3.

</details>

### Q12. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct order of reactivity of $\mathrm{CH}_{3} \mathrm{Br}$ in methanol with the following nucleophiles is $\mathrm{F}^{-}, \mathrm{I}^{-}, \mathrm{C}_{2} \mathrm{H}_{5} \mathrm{O}^{-}$and $\mathrm{C}_{6} \mathrm{H}_{5} \mathrm{O}^{-}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{I}^{-}>\mathrm{C}_{2} \mathrm{H}_{5} \mathrm{O}^{-}>\mathrm{F}^{-}>\mathrm{C}_{6} \mathrm{H}_{5} \mathrm{O}^{-}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{I}^{-}>\mathrm{C}_{2} \mathrm{H}_{5} \mathrm{O}^{-}>\mathrm{C}_{6} \mathrm{H}_{5} \mathrm{O}^{-}>\mathrm{F}^{-}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{I}^{-}>\mathrm{F}^{-}>\mathrm{C}_{6} \mathrm{H}_{5} \mathrm{O}^{-}>\mathrm{C}_{2} \mathrm{H}_{5} \mathrm{O}^{-}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{I}^{-}>\mathrm{C}_{6} \mathrm{H}_{5} \mathrm{O}^{-}>\mathrm{F}^{-}>\mathrm{C}_{2} \mathrm{H}_{5} \mathrm{O}^{-}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{I}^{-}>\mathrm{C}_{2} \mathrm{H}_{5} \mathrm{O}^{-}>\mathrm{C}_{6} \mathrm{H}_{5} \mathrm{O}^{-}>\mathrm{F}^{-}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In methanol (protic solvent), nucleophilicity is determined by basicity and solvation effects. Smaller ions are heavily solvated by the protic solvent, reducing their effective nucleophilicity.

C₂H₅O⁻ (ethoxide) is the strongest nucleophile as it is less solvated and highly basic.

C₆H₅O⁻ (phenoxide) is weaker due to resonance stabilization by the benzene ring.

F⁻ is highly solvated despite high basicity, reducing its nucleophilicity significantly.

I⁻ is the best nucleophile because it is the largest ion and least solvated in methanol.

The correct order is I⁻ > C₂H₅O⁻ > C₆H₅O⁻ > F⁻.

</details>

### Q13. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct order of the rate of reaction of the following reactants with nucleophile by SN1 mechanism is:
(Given : Structure I and II are rigid)
<img style="width:300px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/22S1_q_51_1_v2.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** III $<$ I $<$ II $<$ IV
- **(B)** IV $<$ III $<$ II $<$ I
- **(C)** I $<$ II $<$ III $<$ IV
- **(D)** II $<$ I $<$ III $<$ IV

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) II $<$ I $<$ III $<$ IV**

**Detailed Solution:**

Rate of $S_N1 \propto$ Stability of $C^{\oplus}$ formed
(I) and (II) are unstable due to Bredt's rule, (I) has more +I effect.
(II) < (I) < (III) < (IV)

</details>

### Q14. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct order of reactivity of the following benzyl halides towards reaction with KCN is : 
 <img style="width:400px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/21S2_q_68_1.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $b>a>c>d$
- **(B)** $b>a>d>c$
- **(C)** $a>b>d>c$
- **(D)** $a>b>c>d$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $b>a>d>c$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Reactivity in SN2 reactions with KCN depends on the stability and nucleophilicity of the transition state.
For benzyl halides, substituents on the benzene ring affect the reactivity of the benzylic carbon through resonance and inductive effects. 
Para-nitro group (-NO2) is strongly electron-withdrawing, activating the benzylic carbon toward nucleophilic substitution. 
Para-amino (-NH2) is electron-donating, deactivating. 
Para-hydroxyl (-OH) is weakly donating. 
Meta-nitro group has reduced electron-withdrawal compared to para-position. 
Order of reactivity based on electronic effects: para-NO2 (c) > meta-NO2 (d) > para-OH (a) > para-NH2 (b). 
This gives b > a > d > c

</details>

### Q15. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct order of the rate of the reaction for the following reaction with respect to nucleophiles is : $\mathrm{CH}_{3} \mathrm{Br}+\mathrm{Nu}^{\ominus} \longrightarrow \mathrm{CH}_{3} \mathrm{Nu}+\mathrm{Br}^{\ominus}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{PhO}^{-}>{ }^{-} \mathrm{OH}>\mathrm{CH}_{3} \mathrm{COO}^{-}>\mathrm{ClO}_{4}^{-}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{CH}_{3} \mathrm{COO}^{-}>\mathrm{PhO}^{-}>{ }^{-} \mathrm{OH}>\mathrm{ClO}_{4}^{-}$
- **(C)** ${ }^{-} \mathrm{OH}>\mathrm{PhO}^{-}>\mathrm{CH}_{3} \mathrm{COO}^{-}>\mathrm{ClO}_{4}^{-}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{ClO}_{4}^{-}>\mathrm{CH}_{3} \mathrm{COO}^{-}>{ }^{-} \mathrm{OH}>\mathrm{PhO}^{-}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) ${ }^{-} \mathrm{OH}>\mathrm{PhO}^{-}>\mathrm{CH}_{3} \mathrm{COO}^{-}>\mathrm{ClO}_{4}^{-}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For the SN2 reaction CH₃Br + Nu⁻, nucleophilicity in aprotic solvents is determined by basicity and polarizability.
⁻OH (hydroxide) is the strongest nucleophile and base, making it highly reactive.
PhO⁻ (phenoxide) is a good nucleophile with reasonable basicity.
CH₃COO⁻ (acetate) is weakened by resonance stabilization of its negative charge across the C-O bonds, reducing its nucleophilicity.
ClO₄⁻ (perchlorate) is a very weak nucleophile, being the conjugate base of a strong acid (HClO₄).
The correct order of nucleophilicity is: ⁻OH > PhO⁻ > CH₃COO⁻ > ClO₄⁻, which matches option (3).

</details>

### Q22. JEE Main 2025 (22 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The compound with molecular formula $\mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_6$, which gives only one monobromo derivative and takes up four moles of hydrogen per mole for complete hydrogenation has ________ $=$ electrons.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **8**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since 4 moles of $\mathrm{H}_2$ is being added for complete hydrogenation the degree of unsaturation $=4$

No. of $\pi$ electrons in $\mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_6=4 \times 2=8$

</details>

Reason (R) : The partially bonded unhybridized p-orbital that develops in the trigonal bipyramidal transition state is stabilized by conjugation with the phenyl ring.

- **(A)** (A) is correct but (R) is not correct
- **(B)** (A) is not correct but (R) is correct

### Q32. JEE Main 2024 (04 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Identify the correct set of reagents or reaction conditions ' \(X\) ' and ' \(Y\) ' in the following set of transformation
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/cECwq_evBPJH-s5w1BmeQd07R-XFRku59a2r_KEgjlw.original.fullsize.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** \(\mathrm{X}=\) dil.aq. \(\mathrm{NaOH}, 20^{\circ} \mathrm{C}, \quad \mathrm{Y}=\mathrm{Br}_2 / \mathrm{CHCl}_3\)
- **(B)** \(\mathrm{X}=\) conc.alc. \(\mathrm{NaOH}, 80^{\circ} \mathrm{C}, \mathrm{Y}=\mathrm{Br}_2 / \mathrm{CHCl}_3\)
- **(C)** \(\mathrm{X}=\) dil.aq. \(\mathrm{NaOH}, 20^{\circ} \mathrm{C}, \quad \mathrm{Y}=\mathrm{HBr}\) /acetic acid
- **(D)** \(\mathrm{X}=\) conc.alc. \(\mathrm{NaOH}, 80^{\circ} \mathrm{C}, \mathrm{Y}=\mathrm{HBr} /\) acetic acid

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) \(\mathrm{X}=\) conc.alc. \(\mathrm{NaOH}, 80^{\circ} \mathrm{C}, \mathrm{Y}=\mathrm{HBr} /\) acetic acid**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{CH}_3-\mathrm{CH}_2-\mathrm{CH}_2-\mathrm{Br} \xrightarrow{\mathrm{X}=\text { conc.alc. } \mathrm{NaOH}}$
$\mathrm{CH}_3-\mathrm{CH}=\mathrm{CH}_2 \xrightarrow{\mathrm{Y} \equiv \mathrm{HBr} / \text { Acetic acid }} \mathrm{CH}_3-\mathrm{CHBr}-\mathrm{CH}_3$

</details>

### Q33. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d8d8657d-e5c2-4f0b-b7ff-dbe180dc0d56.png" style="width: 500px; height: 115px;"/></p><p>Acid <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math> formed in above reaction is:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Gluconic acid
- **(B)** Succinic acid
- **(C)** Oxalic acid
- **(D)** Malonic acid

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Succinic acid**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The ethyl bromide undergo elimination reaction with alc.KOH gives ethene.</p><p>Ethene gives dibromoethane with bromine in carbon tetra chloride. The dibromoethane converts to dicyanoethane with excess KCN. The dicyanoethane on hydrolysis gives Succinic Acid.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/48837356-4a3a-47bb-a9df-b0756ca8203d.png" style="width: 755px; height: 394px;"/></p>

</details>

---

### Q34. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Identify <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> in the following sequence of reaction</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/59d9c283-c6e9-4ca1-84db-57aefdc629fd.png" style="width: 600px; height: 174px;"/></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d7b88608-f5bb-4cb9-972e-46e9ab22ef7e.png" style="width: 444px; height: 109px;"/>
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c5c7e717-c673-4e99-9b30-44737a4e92d8.png" style="width: 444px; height: 112px;"/>
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9b117418-d6cf-432a-93af-d2bccf90c675.png" style="width: 441px; height: 111px;"/>
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ad1bb8b5-f30b-45a3-aac1-061b5d031a1c.png" style="width: 435px; height: 108px;"/>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c5c7e717-c673-4e99-9b30-44737a4e92d8.png" style="width: 444px; height: 112px;"/>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Toluene reacts with chlorine in the presence of light to give Benzal chloride. The reaction happens between methylbenzene and chlorine in the presence of ultraviolet light - typically sunlight. This is a good example of a photochemical reaction - a reaction brought about by light.</p><p>Benzyl chloride on reaction with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>/</mo><mn>373</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> gives benzaldehyde.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/cf7df457-312f-4d4f-b6ea-fda7974a8343.png" style="width: 900px; height: 246px;"/></p>

</details>

### Q35. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements : one is labelled as Assertion <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> and the other is labelled as Reason <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Assertion <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>:</mo></math> Haloalkanes react with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KCN</mi></math> to form alkyl cyanides as a main product while with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AgCN</mi></math> form isocyanide as the main product.</p><p>Reason <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced><mo>:</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KCN</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AgCN</mi></math> both are highly ionic compounds.</p><p>In the light of the above statement, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> is correct but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> is not correct
- **(B)** Both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> are correct but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> is not the correct explanation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> is not correct but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> is correct
- **(D)** Both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> are correct and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> is the correct explanation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> is correct but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> is not correct**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KCN</mi></math> is ionic in nature and provides cyanide ions in solution. Although both carbon and nitrogen atoms are in a position to donate electron pairs, the attack takes place mainly
through carbon atom and not through nitrogen atom since <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> bond is more stable than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> bond.
Involved reaction is shown below:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mover><mrow><mover><mo>→</mo><mrow><mi>Substitution</mi><mo> </mo><mi>reaction</mi></mrow></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>CN</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>KX</mi></mrow><mrow></mrow></mover><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi>Alkyl</mi><mo> </mo><mi>halide</mi><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mi>Cyanide</mi></math>
Reaction of alkyl halide with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AgCN</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AgCN</mi></math> is mainly covalent in nature. Here only nitrogen is free to donate electron pairs,hence it forms isocyanide as the main product.
Involved reaction is shown below:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>AgCN</mi><mover><mo>→</mo><mrow><mi>Substitution</mi><mo> </mo><mi>reaction</mi></mrow></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>NC</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>AgX</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi>Alkyl</mi><mo> </mo><mi>halide</mi><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mi>Isocyanide</mi></math>
Hence, here isocyanide is formed as major product.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/512a6ca6-4638-45b6-af69-96b86c7f7aba.png" style="width: 550px; height: 265px;"/></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AgCN</mi></math> is mainly covalent in nature and nitrogen is available for attack, so alkyl isocyanide is formed as main product.</p><p>Cyanide ion is an ambidentate ligand (nucleophile or lewis base). It means that donation of a pair of electrons can be through either carbon or nitrogen, so it has two nucleophilic centres. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KCN</mi></math> is predominantly ionic and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AgCN</mi></math> is predominantly covalent.</p>

</details>

### Q36. JEE Main 2024 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c453ca27-25c0-4c96-9301-acedee68d939.png" style="width: 650px; height: 122px;"/></p><p>The total number of hydrogen atoms in product <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> and product <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is__________.</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **10**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>When bromoethane reacts with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math> in the presence of ethyl alcohol gives Ethene. </p><p>Similarly When bromoethane reacts with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math> in the aqueous medium gives Ethyl alcohol. This reaction is an example of a nucleophilic substitution reaction.</p><p>The reaction is as follows,</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d889565c-7948-4b51-92bc-86b1ce37497c.png" style="width: 700px; height: 118px;"/></p><p>Total number of hydrogen atom in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> that is Ethene and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> that is  Ethyl alcohol is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q37. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>chlorobutane</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>→</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>8</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> (isomers)
Total number of optically active isomers shown by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>8</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>, obtained in the above reaction is ________.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Optical activity is the ability of a substance to rotate the plane of polarisation of a beam of light that is passed through it.A compound to be said as optically active is only if it has to have an asymmetric chiral carbon centre (the carbon is said to be chiral if they have different substituents attached).</p><p>The optically active substances that rotate the plane of polarisation of the light towards the right are known as right-handed or dextrorotatory. It can be Indicated by including (+) or ‘D’ before the compound name.</p><p>Substances that rotate the plane of polarisation of the light toward the left are known as left-handed or laevorotatory. It can be Indicated by including (-) or ‘L’ before the compound name</p><p>The reaction is as follows,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>chlorobutane</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>→</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>8</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> </p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>8</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> will give four isomers of which one is optically inactive. Their structures are as follows,</p><p> </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0df55944-2c39-4e2b-a823-35df9ce41de8.png" style="width: 500px; height: 336px;"/>Here only two, i.e., II and IV are optically active. Since each optically active compound has two enantiomers, therefore, total <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn></math> isomeric compounds are possible.</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q38. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements:
Statement - I: High concentration of strong nucleophilic reagent with secondary alkyl halides which do not have bulky substituents will follow <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> mechanism.
Statement - II: A secondary alkyl halide when treated with a large excess of ethanol follows <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> mechanism.
In the the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate from the questions given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false.
- **(B)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true.
- **(C)** Both statement I and Statement II are false.
- **(D)** Both statement I and Statement II are true.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Both statement I and Statement II are true.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Statement - I: Rate of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn><mo>∝</mo><mo>[</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>]</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><msup><mi>Nu</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mfenced></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> reaction is favoured by high concentration of nucleophile <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msup><mi>Nu</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mfenced><mo>&amp;</mo></math> less crowding in the substrate molecule.</p><h2><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> Reaction Mechanism</h2><p>This reaction proceeds through a backside attack by the nucleophile on the substrate. The nucleophile approaches the given substrate at an angle of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>180</mn><mo>°</mo></msup></math> to the carbon-leaving group bond. The carbon-nucleophile bond forms and carbon-leaving group <em>bond breaks </em>simultaneously through a transition state.</p><p>Now, the leaving group is pushed out of the transition state on the opposite side of the carbon-nucleophile bond, forming the required product. It is important to note that the <em>product is formed with an inversion of the tetrahedral geometry at the atom in the centre.</em></p><p>Statement - II: Solvolysis follows <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> path.</p><p>he <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> reaction is a nucleophilic substitution reaction where the rate-determining step is unimolecular. It is a type of organic substitution reaction. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> stands for substitution nucleophilic unimolecular. Thus, the rate equation (which states that the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> reaction is dependent on the electrophile but not on the nucleophile) holds in situations where the amount of the nucleophile is far greater than the amount of the carbocation intermediate.</p><p>This reaction involves the formation of a carbocation intermediate. It is generally seen in the reactions of tertiary or secondary alkyl halides with secondary or tertiary alcohols under strongly acidic or strongly basic conditions. The <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> reaction is often referred to as the dissociative mechanism in inorganic chemistry.</p><p>Both are correct statements.</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q39. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Alkyl halide is converted into alkyl isocyanide by reaction with

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaCN</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mi>CN</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KCN</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AgCN</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AgCN</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AgCN</mi></math> is covalent in nature so it does not dissociate in to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>CN</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> and on nitrogen electron density is high,and nitrogen is free to donate electron pair forming isocyanide or alkyl isonitrile as the main product.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>AgCN</mi><mo>→</mo><mi>RNC</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>AgX</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KCN</mi></math> is ionic in nature, so it dissociates completely.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>KCN</mi><mo>→</mo><mi>RCN</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>KX</mi></math></p><p>Hence the answer is D.</p>

</details>

---

### Q40. JEE Main 2024 (27 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>Identify B formed in the reaction.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>Cl</mi><mover><mo>→</mo><mrow><mi>excess</mi><mo> </mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mover><mo>→</mo><mi>NaOH</mi></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>NaCl</mi></math></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/2055c171-22c8-4042-83e5-871e453b047b.png" style="width: 150px; height: 118px;"/>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><maction actiontype="argument"><mrow></mrow></maction><mo>-</mo></msup><msub><mi>ClH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>-</mo><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>+</mo></mover><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/78470601-ab71-4cc2-bbdb-61fdb14bed0a.png" style="width: 160px; height: 263px;"/>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Substituted ammonium salt is formed when chloroalkane reacted with excess of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>, which on reaction with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math> gives <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>diaminobutane</mi></math>.</p><p>The carbon - halogen bond in alkyl halides can be easily cleaved by a nucleophile. Hence, an alkyl halide on reaction with an ethanolic solution of ammonia undergoes a nucleophilic substitution reaction in which the halogen atom is replaced by an amino group. This process of cleavage of the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>–</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> bond by ammonia molecule is known as ammonolysis.  Substituted ammonium salt on reaction with sodium hydroxide gives 1,4-diaminobutane as shown below,</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/08d94b82-16e7-478f-92e5-17c71760cfb9.png" style="width: 700px; height: 314px;"/></p><p>Hence, the answer is option B.</p>

</details>

---

### Q41. JEE Main 2024 (27 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which among the following halide/s will not show <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> reaction:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>CH</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>Cl</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>CH</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>CH</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>Cl</mi></math>
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8df0e99e-9c9a-4e90-a6c5-bf81c54e1543.png" style="width: 300px; height: 149px;"/>
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b689e41c-b55d-4b10-b79e-709c988b272a.png" style="width: 290px; height: 174px;"/>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>CH</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>CH</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>Cl</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Vinylic halides are organic compounds containing their halogen atom bonded to one of the two carbon atoms in the double bond present in that molecule.</p><p>Vinyl halide will not show <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> reaction, due to very
less stability of carbocation formed and partial
double bond character in C — Cl bond.</p><p>Since <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>CH</mi><mo>=</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>+</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math> is very unstable, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>CH</mi><mo>=</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CH</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>Cl</mi></math> cannot give <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> reaction.</p><p>Hence, the answer is B.</p>

</details>

---

### Q42. JEE Main 2024 (27 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The correct statement regarding nucleophilic substitution reaction in a chiral alkyl halide is ;

**Options:**

- **(A)** Retention occurs in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mo> </mo></math>reaction and inversion occurs in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> reaction.
- **(B)** Racemisation occurs in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mo> </mo></math>reaction and retention occurs in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> reaction.
- **(C)** Racemisation occurs in both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> reactions.
- **(D)** Racemisation occurs in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> reaction and inversion occurs in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> reaction.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Racemisation occurs in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> reaction and inversion occurs in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> reaction.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In the 1<sup>st</sup> step of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> mechanism a carbocation is formed which is planar .Therefore the nucleophile can attack the carbocation from both the sides. When a nucleophile attacks the carbocation from the side where initially halogen was there,the product with retention in configuration is produce and when nucleophile attacks the carbocation from backside, the product with inversion in configuration is produce. Hence <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn></math>% of the product will be with retention in configuration and rest <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn></math>% will be with inversion in configuration. This produces a racemic mixture which is optically inactive.</p><p>In <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> reaction mechanism requires the attack of nucleophile from the back side of the carbon atom. So the product assumes a stereochemical position opposite to the leaving group originally occupied. This is called inversion of configuration.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> – Racemisation</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> – Inversion</p><p>Hence option D is the answer.</p>

</details>

### Q43. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>Match List-I with List-II.</p><p>1-Bromopropane is reacted with reagents in List-I to give product in List-II</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td> </td>			<td>List-I Reagent</td>			<td> </td>			<td>List-II Product</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>A.</td>			<td>KOH (alc)</td>			<td>I.</td>			<td>Nitrile</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>B.</td>			<td>KCN (alc)</td>			<td>II.</td>			<td>Ester</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>C.</td>			<td>AgNO<sub>2</sub></td>			<td>III.</td>			<td>Alkene</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>D</td>			<td>H<sub>3</sub>CCOOAg</td>			<td>IV.</td>			<td>Nitroalkane</td>		</tr>	</tbody></table>Choose the correct answer from the options given below<p> </p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-III, B-I, C-IV, D-II
- **(B)** A-I, B-II, C-III, D-IV
- **(C)** A-I, B-III, C-IV, D-II
- **(D)** A-IV, B-III, C-II, D-I

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) A-III, B-I, C-IV, D-II**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>(A) When <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo></math>bromopropane reacts with alcoholic<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KOH</mi></math>, the bromine with the alpha H (hydrogen from the carbon alongside the carbon fortified with useful gathering) is eliminated and subsequently forms <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo></math>propene. <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/af89f5c7-a461-4945-8f7d-271ad15a79f9.png" style="width: 300px; height: 61px;" /></p><p>(B) When <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo></math>Bromo propane reacts with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>alc</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KCN</mi></math> it will form butanenitrile. </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f5e4c9d5-301c-4f08-acfc-26ebbdaaee6e.png" style="width: 300px; height: 63px;" /></p><p>(C)  When akyl halide reacts with silver nitrite, nitroalkane is formed because the bond between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ag</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi>OAg</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> is covalent, the lone pair on nitrogen acts as an attacking site for nucleophilic substitution.  <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4d950621-18cc-4e64-bf3b-d96e81fbabf9.png" style="width: 300px; height: 80px;" /></p><p>(D) When <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo></math>bromopropane reacts with silver acetate, propyl ethanoate and silver bromide are formed.<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bf11b6d8-cd01-408d-9650-69d11993fb7d.png" style="width: 300px; height: 85px;" /></p>

</details>

---

### Q44. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></math>Methyl propyl bromide reacts with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>and gives <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8216;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#8217;</mo></math> whereas on reaction with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi></math> it gives <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8216;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#8217;</mo><mo>.</mo></math> The mechanism followed in these reactions and the products <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8216;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#8217;</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8216;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#8217;</mo></math> respectively are:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>=</mo></math>iso-butyl ethyl ether;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>=</mo></math>tert-butyl ethyl ether</p>
- **(B)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>=</mo></math>tert-butyl ethyl ether; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></math>butyl ethyl ether</p>
- **(C)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></math>butyl ethyl ether; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>=</mo></math>iso-butyl ethyl ether</p>
- **(D)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>=</mo></math>tert-butyl ethyl ether; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>=</mo></math>iso-butyl ethyl ether</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>=</mo></math>iso-butyl ethyl ether;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>=</mo></math>tert-butyl ethyl ether</p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The reaction between methyl propyl bromide and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> proceeds via an <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> (substitution nucleophilic bimolecular) mechanism. In this mechanism, the nucleophile attacks the carbon atom bearing the bromine (the electrophilic carbon) in a single step, leading to the displacement of the bromine atom.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/56eaaed5-8fc6-4c4a-8fb2-4ff7f7beabee.png" style="width: 500px; height: 126px;" /></p><p>The reaction between methyl propyl bromide and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi></math> can proceed via either an <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> (substitution nucleophilic unimolecular). Here <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi></math> is a weak nucleophile. During the reaction 1,2-hydrogen shift will occur and tert-butyl ethyl ether is formed.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f1d0ecef-f74f-4ec6-9967-5e44c2d2f3f3.png" style="width: 600px; height: 218px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q45. JEE Main 2023 (10 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>The major product &lsquo;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>&rsquo; formed in the given reaction is</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a1860188-94ad-425e-9c5f-0c94c8c2ca61.png" style="width: 400px; height: 106px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/68c4edb9-e89f-45d1-8e62-19a6008f8671.png" style="width: 250px; height: 165px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/28de690a-987c-4c32-888b-08c1426fd418.png" style="width: 350px; height: 139px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5231deda-e751-4ce8-a28d-8434ee9c2018.png" style="width: 200px; height: 168px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/604495e9-cbc6-4fdb-af6d-68d8d3dcb595.png" style="width: 250px; height: 106px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/68c4edb9-e89f-45d1-8e62-19a6008f8671.png" style="width: 250px; height: 165px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Friedel-Crafts Alkylation refers to the replacement of an aromatic proton with an alkyl group. This is done through an electrophilic attack on the aromatic ring with the help of a carbocation generated from the alkyl halide and Lewis acid aluminium chloride. In the given case ring closure reaction takes place and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>OCH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is ortho/para directing, major product will be obtained from para attack.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5efe8fc5-89be-437d-82d7-2499d824c2a0.png" style="width: 500px; height: 152px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q46. JEE Main 2023 (10 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>Identify the correct order of reactivity for the following pairs towards the respective mechanism</p><p>(A) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/844e701c-5bda-4bb6-ae13-c5e45cf238ed.png" style="width: 362px; height: 84px;" /></p><p>(B) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/53a278f5-e816-4127-97c4-b450793584e3.png" style="width: 500px; height: 121px;" /></p><p>(C) Electrophilic substitution</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7e5153f1-9942-445f-8eae-5ca54803b3e1.png" style="width: 200px; height: 185px;" /></p><p>(D) Nucleophilic substitution</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ecb7da47-4e07-4837-9588-91d2b43d80fa.png" style="width: 250px; height: 180px;" /></p><p>Choose the correction answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced></math> only</p><p> </p>
- **(B)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced></math></p><p> </p>
- **(C)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced></math> only</p><p> </p><p> </p>
- **(D)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced></math>only</p><p> </p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced></math></p><p> </p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The rate of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> reaction decreases with increase in steric crowding. In the given set of molecules the second molecule(Tertiary alkyl halide) is sterically crowded, hence, it is correct option. More stable carbocation formed, higher will be the reactivity of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math>. Benzyl carbocation is more reactive than aliphatic carbocation.</p><p>The deactivating groups decreases the rate of electrophilic substitution reaction. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is strong deactivating group for electrophilic substitution.</p><p>In the aromatic nucleophilic substitution reactions, the intermediate formed is carbanion and, it is stabilised by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> at ortho or para positions.</p><p>Hence <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math> all are correct.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q47. JEE Main 2023 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Choose the halogen which is most reactive towards SN1 reaction in the given compounds (A, B, C &amp; D)</p><p>A. <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6f06b262-7155-41aa-bb1a-4ab072dca3c0.png" style="width: 200px; height: 146px;" /></p><p>B. <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bc464402-0744-4ff9-8362-3e7ce7134916.png" style="width: 250px; height: 54px;" /></p><p>C. <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5c8d8b32-a2b3-4f7e-9539-485e2c0c12bf.png" style="width: 200px; height: 150px;" /></p><p>D. <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/06c7a3e2-2c93-4164-9a46-4431a3814ecb.png" style="width: 218px; height: 108px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>Br</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>Br</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>Br</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The leaving group which results in the formation of more stable carbocation will be more reactive towards S<sub>N</sub>1 reaction.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6f06b262-7155-41aa-bb1a-4ab072dca3c0.png" style="width: 200px; height: 146px;" /></p><p>In the above molecule, by leaving of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub></math> benzyl carbocation is formed. Benzyl carbocation is more stable than aliphatic carbocation.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bc464402-0744-4ff9-8362-3e7ce7134916.png" style="width: 250px; height: 54px;" /></p><p>By replacing <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub></math> in the above molecule, allyl carbocation is formed. Allyl carbocation is more stable than aliphatic carbocation.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5c8d8b32-a2b3-4f7e-9539-485e2c0c12bf.png" style="width: 200px; height: 150px;" /></p><p>In the above molecule, it is difficult to replace <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub></math>, as carbocation formed at bridge head position is very difficult. Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub></math> is more reactive.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/06c7a3e2-2c93-4164-9a46-4431a3814ecb.png" style="width: 218px; height: 108px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub></math> is more reactive than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub></math> in the above molecule, because tertiary carbocation is more stable than primary carbocation.</p><p>Hence, the correct answer is option (3).</p><p> </p></blockquote>

</details>
