### Q48. JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>For the reaction</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>RCH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>Br</mi><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mover><mo>&#10230;</mo><mtext>&#160;Acetone&#160;</mtext></mover><munder><mrow><msub><mi>RCH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mtext>&#160;major&#160;</mtext></munder><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>Br</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math></p><p>The correct statement is</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Br</mi><mo>&#8211;</mo></msup></math> can act as competing nucleophile.</p>
- **(B)** <p>The reaction can occur in acetic acid also.</p>
- **(C)** <p>The transition state formed in the above reaction is less polar than the localised anion.</p>
- **(D)** <p>The solvent used in the reaction solvates the ions formed in rate determining step</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <p>The transition state formed in the above reaction is less polar than the localised anion.</p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>Br</mi><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mover><mo>&#10230;</mo><mtext>&#160;Acetone&#160;</mtext></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>Br</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math></p><p>As mentioned the reaction follows <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math>  reaction mechanism. The Finkelstein reaction classically involves the conversion of Alkyl bromides into Alkyl iodides by the treatment with a solution of Sodium iodide in Acetone. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaBr</mi></math> is not readily soluble in acetone and hence reaction shifts in forward reaction. For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> reactions, transition state formed is less polar than the localised anion.</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q49. JEE Main 2023 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>Decreasing order towards <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> reaction for the following compounds is:</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/28897d63-6e27-4d6d-a6c2-2de9fa2e91c5-9895989.png" style="width: 500px; height: 263px;" /> </p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** a &gt; c &gt; d &gt; b
- **(B)** a &gt; b &gt; c &gt; d
- **(C)** b &gt; d &gt; c &gt; a
- **(D)** d &gt; b &gt; c &gt; a

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) b &gt; d &gt; c &gt; a**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The relative reactivity of alkyl halides in the S<sub>N</sub>1 reaction depends on the stability of carbocation. Electron-withdrawing groups decrease the stability and electron-releasing groups increase the stability of carbocations. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> group is an electron-withdrawing group by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> effect. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mi>OMe</mi></math> is electron-releasing groups by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> effect. Hence, the overall order is </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f220809f-4a96-4218-a96c-5d98f428425e-9895989_2.png" style="width: 500px; height: 241px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> Reactivity order (b) &gt; (d) &gt; (c) &gt; (a)</p>

</details>

### Q50. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The one giving maximum number of isomeric alkenes on dehydrohalogenation reaction is (excluding rearrangement)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>-Bromo-<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>-methylbutane
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>-Bromopropane
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>-Bromopentane
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>-Bromo-<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math>-dimethylpentane

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>-Bromopentane**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In dehydrohalogenation reactions, the preferred product is that alkene which has the greater number of alkyl groups attached to the doubly bonded carbon atoms. Thus, 2-bromopentane gives pent-2-ene as the major product.</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/83488d5f-42a2-4f47-9bed-c1a7ad154c16-7fc755fa-a43e-49f8-9496-f41827617b4f-a44d5f49-5106-4827-8a0f-ce21c19634a1-image.png" style="width: 446px; height: 103px;" />Rest all bromides are having only one type of beta hydrogen, hence, only one possible alkene is formed.</p>

</details>

### Q51. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>Identify the correct order for the given property for following compounds</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/855424b2-100c-4185-91d8-9fa6439e83ac-image.png" style="width: 442px; height: 228px;" /></p><p>Choose the correct answer from the option given below :-</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** (B), (C) and (D) only
- **(B)** (A), (C) and (E) only
- **(C)** (A), (C) and (D) only
- **(D)** (A), (B) and (E) only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) (A), (C) and (E) only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The boiling point of organic compounds depends on two factors:</p><p>(i) Molecular weight: Boiling point increases with increase in molecular weight.</p><p>(ii) Intermolecular forces: Higher the strength of intermolecular forces, higher is the boiling point.</p><p>As the mass of the compound increases then their boiling point will also increase. Hence, (A) and (C) are correct.</p><p>Density of Et-Cl = 0.89 g/ml</p><p>                 Et-Br = 1.47 g/ml</p><p>                 Et-I = 1.94 g/ml</p><p>Density of halides increases with number of carbon atoms and at mass of halogen present. So, (B) and (D) is incorrect.</p><p>As branching of the compound increases then boiling point decreases. Hence, (E) is correct.</p><p>So, the correct answer is (A), (C) and (E) only.</p><p> </p>

</details>

### Q52. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 2)

Maximum number of isomeric monochloro derivatives which can be obtained from 2,2,5,5- tetramethylhexane by chlorination is _________

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The given alkane 2,2,5,5- tetramethylhexane has two types hydrogen, hence, it forms two different structural isomers on chlorination.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9884fd62-3f7e-43fd-adcc-1797ef236059-83942cca-4909-47ac-96d7-c852de2f9932-9894330.png" style="width: 355px; height: 462px;" /></p><p>Total numbers of isomers are equal to three as second molecule is optically active.</p>

</details>

### Q53. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Number of moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AgCl</mi></math> formed in the following reaction is _____ .</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/db4f8509-4294-49db-b11c-e7a3dc740792-9893988.PNG" style="width: 476px; height: 126px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Benzylic and tertiary carbocations are stable. The positive charge is unstable at double bonded carbon. Hence, two moles of silver chloride is precipitated from one mole of given molecule.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/50a85a70-b5f6-4e5e-9ccf-be2511e246d3-image.png" style="width: 308px; height: 147px;" /></p><p>In the above molecule, carbon-1 and carbo-2 are double bonded carbons. Carbon-3 and carbon-4 forms secondary carbocation and tertiary carbocation respectively.</p>

</details>

### Q54. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>In the following given reaction &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>&#39; is</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bc52b34f-1e6a-4abf-ac1e-8d07456cd37c-9893942.PNG" style="width: 411px; height: 130px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/59d0ade7-07cf-429e-98e0-08aa77c28764-9893942_1.PNG" style="width: 123px; height: 134px;" />
- **(B)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7229d3d2-bd0e-45d3-8b8d-cdcf39c5b083-9893942_2.PNG" style="width: 140px; height: 150px;" />
- **(C)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d6af8872-ca14-4768-b728-b7b657c35ee0-9893942_3.PNG" style="width: 197px; height: 150px;" />
- **(D)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e5b759be-0e83-4396-a636-fc85e2d7323f-9893942_4.PNG" style="width: 187px; height: 142px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d6af8872-ca14-4768-b728-b7b657c35ee0-9893942_3.PNG" style="width: 197px; height: 150px;" />**

A tertiary carbocation is generated undergo ring expansion as five membered rings is more stable than four membered rings.</p><p> </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/09241153-47d1-462c-9cfd-c185d91b1055-image.png" style="width: 625px; height: 270px;" /></p>

### Q55. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p><strong>Assertion A</strong>: Hydrolysis of an alkyl chloride is a slow reaction but in the presence of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaI</mi></math>, the rate of hydrolysis increases.</p><p><strong>Reason R</strong>: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math><sup> </sup>is a good nucleophile as well as a good leaving group.</p><p>In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A is false but R is true
- **(B)** A is true but R is false
- **(C)** Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A
- **(D)** Both A and R are true and R is not the correct explanation of A

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Iodide ion is a powerful nucleophile and hence reacts rapidly with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>RCl</mi></math> to form <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>RI</mi></math>. Further because <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo></math>ion is a better leaving group than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo></msup></math> ion, therefore , <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>RI</mi></math> is more rapidly hydrolysed than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>RCl</mi></math> to form <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ROH</mi></math>.</p>

</details>

---

### Q56. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>Compound &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>&#39; undergoes following sequence of reactions to give compound &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>&#39;. The correct structure and chirality of compound &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>&#39; is [where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Et</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></math>]</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8d19d13b-3959-418c-ae9c-02ca80e4f47e-image.png" style="width: 251px; height: 70px;" />​​​​​​​</p><p>Compound &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>&#39;</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/dafb5141-f34b-4449-8134-7be5c2856051-image.png" style="width: 164px; height: 60px;" /></p>
- **(B)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b669d451-6df5-4dbf-8803-8ea61de4d934-image.png" style="width: 170px; height: 60px;" /></p>
- **(C)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a4bccdf6-4370-442b-89ad-6330e30139cb-image.png" style="width: 166px; height: 60px;" /></p>
- **(D)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5e7a4018-3cb9-40f6-bf22-7bc646ba4d10-image.png" style="width: 236px; height: 60px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a4bccdf6-4370-442b-89ad-6330e30139cb-image.png" style="width: 166px; height: 60px;" /></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>In the first step the given alkyl halide that is 2-bromo-3-methyl butane reacts with Mg in presence of dry ether to form a grignard reagent,This grignard reagent reacts with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> to form a hydrocarbon (B)which has a  chiral carbon.</p><p>In the reaction with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> grignard reagent behaves as a strong base.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/707bd95b-2ecd-4fe9-a95b-629827e5a6c2-24-08-2022.png" style="width: 450px; height: 175px;" /></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q57. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/13d28648-1aba-49c9-9356-bb6fea157e12-image.png" style="width: 656px; height: 70px;" /></p><p>Considering the above reactions, the compound &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>&#39; and compound &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>&#39; respectively are</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f8fedb5c-1212-4b5c-996a-fd59f6d0f958-image.png" style="width: 254px; height: 40px;" /></p>
- **(B)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/20a83a95-d9f8-4759-bedc-cbcbcb0868d5-image.png" style="width: 254px; height: 40px;" /></p>
- **(C)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e766ceba-526f-4c84-8234-161c2a0bbaf2-image.png" style="width: 285px; height: 45px;" /></p>
- **(D)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a3013555-ce98-472f-bda5-0dce4e971058-image.png" style="width: 277px; height: 45px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e766ceba-526f-4c84-8234-161c2a0bbaf2-image.png" style="width: 285px; height: 45px;" /></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Groups like cyanides and nitrites possess two nucleophilic centres and are called ambident nucleophiles. Actually cyanide group is a hybrid of two contributing structures and therefore can act as a nucleophile in two different ways <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mo>[</mo><mo>-</mo></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8596;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>]</mo></math>, i.e., linking through carbon atom resulting in alkyl cyanides and through nitrogen atom leading to isocyanides.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4f92421c-6d12-43bd-af50-6913177e8195-image.png" style="width: 372px; height: 120px;" /></p><p>KCN is predominantly ionic and provides cyanide ions in solution. Although both carbon and nitrogen atoms are in a position to donate electron pairs, the attack takes place mainly through carbon atom and not through nitrogen atom since C&mdash;C bond is more stable than C&mdash;N bond. However, AgCN is mainly covalent in nature and nitrogen is free to donate electron pair forming isocyanide as the main product.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q58. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The structure of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> in the given reaction is</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a3d27286-2a77-493e-94cf-fb8e9aaa680c-image.png" style="width: 318px; height: 90px;" />​​​​​​​</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3bb8fa87-e55e-4b11-a495-ff109b5f7209-image.png" style="width: 140px; height: 120px;" /></p>
- **(B)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8f0dcfd1-2557-4d56-ac6c-4588a459e75c-image.png" style="width: 130px; height: 120px;" /></p>
- **(C)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/312c2f6f-be24-45c7-8687-0a5dd1bdd58a-image.png" style="width: 141px; height: 80px;" /></p>
- **(D)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/34959885-b4c1-46cc-b857-70422fd8de34-image.png" style="width: 177px; height: 70px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/312c2f6f-be24-45c7-8687-0a5dd1bdd58a-image.png" style="width: 141px; height: 80px;" /></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Alpha hydrogen in aldehydes are slightly acidic and in presence of alkali they release <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>ions and carbanion.</p><p>Ethyl bromide undergoes nucleophilic substitution reaction with the carbanion generated, where bromide nucleophile is replaced by the carbanion to form ketone by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> mechansim</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/2a330fe2-0064-4118-8031-777b06eebe56-12-08-2022.png" style="width: 400px; height: 270px;" /></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q59. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Total number of isomers (including stereoisomers) obtain on monochlorination of methylcyclohexane is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **12**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ddf9c7e9-af47-45d3-8d39-99c923ae2175-9-8-2022NEW.png" style="width: 450px; height: 200px;" /></p><p>Compound A and B given above have no chiral centre and doesn&#39;t show geometrical isomerism.</p><p>Compound C and D given above have two chiral centres each and each of them forms 4 optical isomers.</p><p>And compound E has 2 E and Z isomers due to restricted rotation of groups.</p><p>Hence, the total number of  isomers will be 12.</p><p> </p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q60. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements: one is labelled as Assertion <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> and the other is labelled as Reason <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math>.</p><p>Assertion <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> : Experimental reaction of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi>Cl</mi></math> with aniline and anhydrous <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>AlCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> does not give <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></math>-methylaniline.</p><p>Reason <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> : The <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> group of aniline becomes deactivating because of salt formation with anhydrous <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>AlCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> and hence yields <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>-methyl aniline as the product.</p><p>In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> are true and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> is the correct explanation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math>.
- **(B)** Both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> are true but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> is not the correct explanation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math>.
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> is true, but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> is false.
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> is false, but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> is true.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> is true, but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> is false.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Under the Friedel Craft Reaction condition, the aniline (a Lewis base) binds to the electrophile<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>AlCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> (a Lewis acid) to give a coordination complex (a salt). Then, the positive charge on the nitrogen is electron withdrawing, and it pulls the electron density of the aromatic ring by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>effect</mtext></math>. It therefore, deactivates the ring for further alkylation reactions. Finally, the complex precipitates out of the reaction mixture and the alkylation reaction is not observed.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d59d9a23-0911-4ef7-9689-741be11a55d3-image.png" style="width: 342px; height: 180px;" /></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q61. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Most stable product of the following reaction is</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/393898ea-bd91-4dc4-9ba1-bafeb8d78ef5-image.png" style="width: 391px; height: 100px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/fb342175-b046-4dac-aa49-c21c21da35f8-image.png" style="width: 93px; height: 120px;" /></p>
- **(B)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/12e2523a-66e2-4fc7-8bd0-9bfcddd51368-image.png" style="width: 99px; height: 120px;" /></p>
- **(C)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5c75a81a-3134-47ba-988f-def90c99a163-image.png" style="width: 95px; height: 120px;" /></p>
- **(D)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/308b155f-ed9b-48c8-b1c3-9bcff1358f6f-image.png" style="width: 95px; height: 120px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/12e2523a-66e2-4fc7-8bd0-9bfcddd51368-image.png" style="width: 99px; height: 120px;" /></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>This reaction is an example of Darzen&#39;s reaction. It follows <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> mechanism. Hence, inversion product is the major product.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/aa61fd55-3db1-4520-b0d8-a39027f955ab-image.png" style="width: 542px; height: 120px;" /></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q62. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p><img height="76" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/88cdd033-263a-49b6-8f82-7f67d3a891b8-image.png" width="408" /></p><p>The stable carbocation formed in the above reaction is :</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7c36fb91-1e56-4f74-b4be-75d4de12d334-9711836_A.png" style="width: 120px; height: 40px;" /></p>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#8853;</mo></mover><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#8853;</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <p><img height="107" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6a3f25c5-fb94-44b8-b127-ccaa093d4fe8-image.png" width="119" /></p><p> </p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#8853;</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Benzene undergoes Friedel-Crafts Alkylation reaction with propylchloride in presence of anhydrous AlCl<sub>3 </sub>to form isopropyl benzene.</p><p>Propyl carbocation formed undergoes rearrangement to form secondary propyl carbocation</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a6ade6b8-0679-4216-95ae-16c48cc7439e-image.png" style="width: 570px; height: 150px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q63. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>Consider the following reaction,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><munderover><mo>&#8594;</mo><mtable columnalign="left"><mtr><mtd><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>CN</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>/</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo></mtd></mtr></mtable><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><mi>hv</mi></mrow></munderover><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>bromophenylacetic</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>acid</mi></math></p><p>What is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> in the above reaction?</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c055f8c6-cc40-4c0e-83b1-82c48a4980f3-image.png" style="width: 120px; height: 169px;" /></p>
- **(B)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3b299a74-14d6-4269-88f7-fd890e5b6255-image.png" style="width: 90px; height: 136px;" /></p>
- **(C)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/86abd234-f6c6-4c6d-83a9-a55ccd3aeeb9-image.png" style="width: 90px; height: 173px;" /></p>
- **(D)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/79d8dc36-879a-4022-bc00-e1a90195b914-jeeq.png" style="width: 180px; height: 74px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/86abd234-f6c6-4c6d-83a9-a55ccd3aeeb9-image.png" style="width: 90px; height: 173px;" /></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>4-bromotoluene undergoes free radical chlorination,to form 4-bromo benzyl chloride.</p><p>4-bromo benzyl chloride on reaction with KCN from</p><p> 4-Bromobenzyl Cyanide which undergoes hydrolysis to form 4-Bromophenyl acetic acid,</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f72586c6-acd8-47fd-b19c-3994695dfb5a-a2acecf4-8f6d-4f55-8506-4f2010a2d96c-image.png" style="width: 440px; height: 119px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q64. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which one of the following compounds is inactive towards <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> reaction?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bd2e6104-4e32-440e-8201-c6161117dd7a-image.png" style="width: 140px; height: 96px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/da2cd350-7b4c-4b41-af11-aa8a6c0e5a20-image.png" style="width: 130px; height: 67px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/16b4041e-1f34-4b2c-b81d-8e5c4814b54b-image.png" style="width: 130px; height: 103px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/029aedbc-fb34-4d76-8e38-9fccc5fd3901-image.png" style="width: 130px; height: 110px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/16b4041e-1f34-4b2c-b81d-8e5c4814b54b-image.png" style="width: 130px; height: 103px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>SN1 reaction mechanism follows a step-by-step process wherein first, the carbocation is formed from the removal of the leaving group. Then the carbocation is attacked by the nucleophile.</p><p>tertiary, benzylic or allylic carbocations are stable carbocations So, they can give this reaction.</p><p>But Bridge head carbocation formed in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>SN</mi><mn>1</mn></msup></math>pathway is unstable. So, option (C) does not give this reaction.</p>

</details>

<p>The major product <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mfenced></math> in the reaction</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ea073707-c426-4343-b0d8-65e45e4baf65-image.png" style="width: 380px; height: 65px;" /></p>

- **(C)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/73365305-d60a-42a4-acd3-768459b08aa4-9709560_C.png" style="width: 170px; height: 78px;" />
- **(D)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4ead5e11-636b-4a7d-a7db-e29ad7afde8a-9709560_D.png" style="width: 170px; height: 85px;" />

### Q67. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The major product in the reaction</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7f607902-38a7-4560-a70d-6efdc05f5dc2-image.png" style="width: 450px; height: 143px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi></math>-Butyl ethyl ether
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> -Methyl pent-<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>-ene
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></math> -Dimethyl butane
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> -Methyl prop-<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>-ene

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> -Methyl prop-<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>-ene**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> is a very strong base and a weak nucleophile due to its bulkiness. So it will give <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>  elimination reaction as follows.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1cc58184-cda6-4bee-8778-12e8f71c4eb9-3cc07b9a-944f-4118-a1d5-68f3fe903562-image.png" style="width: 670px; height: 147px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q69. JEE Main 2021 (31 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The major product of the following reaction is :
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/919aeb66-364d-4d8c-a9cc-e75bd96f7faf-image.png" style="width: 462px; height: 155px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6ac974cc-1b98-4620-84e9-7ec968e2672a-image.png" style="width: 164px; height: 98px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d5a82390-d276-4f00-954c-2a36d89028fb-image.png" style="width: 154px; height: 103px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/47e7a98f-ed17-44a5-841c-3edd316f1e17-image.png" style="width: 140px; height: 179px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9b2f7bc8-05d9-4276-a8c2-5dca91ee1ba8-image.png" style="width: 148px; height: 143px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d5a82390-d276-4f00-954c-2a36d89028fb-image.png" style="width: 154px; height: 103px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>and</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi></math> is alcoholic<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaOH</mi></math> which is used for dehydrohalogenation of alkenes. This beta elimination is anti-elimination and Saytzeff alkene (more stable) is major product. In the given reaction, more stable conjugated alkene is formed as major product.</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3d9eaf07-3c22-4a05-9867-416710c9ff58-mllaoaop.png" style="width: 462px; height: 155px;" />Note : There is ambiguity in answer given by NTA which is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math>.</p><p> </p>

</details>

- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/050b9844-17da-492b-a3c3-c9b6878420ac-image.png" style="width: 640px; height: 156px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/395344e7-5a92-41e8-86d0-0f4eb6c4209a-image.png" style="width: 616px; height: 157px;" />

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

The given compounds in Acetate in Acetic acid solution reacts through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> mechanism. The stability of carbocation is directly proportion to reactivity via <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> mechanism.

</details>

### Q72. JEE Main 2021 (27 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>In the following sequence of reactions the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math> is:</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c8256a84-a926-46bf-846e-90e67f7e2a92-image.png" style="width: 526px; height: 108px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/459068c6-f9ec-4357-8361-8bd89d7be48d-image.png" style="width: 252px; height: 124px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ed179de9-5f8f-4bd2-8b02-a41a2aaac267-image.png" style="width: 326px; height: 126px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ff534888-f22b-461d-85b2-71e6a06e2595-image.png" style="width: 151px; height: 151px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/2169fa6c-b4f4-4c45-879e-536e34700c00-image.png" style="width: 146px; height: 148px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ff534888-f22b-461d-85b2-71e6a06e2595-image.png" style="width: 151px; height: 151px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>According to the question, In the first step of the reaction, when cyclo-pentyl-chloride reacts with magnesium in the presence of dry ether than cyclo-pentyl-magnesium-chloride (grignard reagent) is formed. </p><p>In the second step of the reaction, when grignard reagent is react with a compound, which has active hydrogen than grignard reagent abstract hydrogen from active hydrogen compound and converted in to alkane. </p><p>So cyclo-pentane is formed as the product(second step of the reaction) of this reaction. </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3945a8a7-cdad-4dd7-956f-53ce630b91f6-image.png" style="width: 576px; height: 94px;" /></p></blockquote>

</details>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>(a) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/048f5f0c-8cf8-4beb-83c6-18ba3fea7141-image.png" style="width: 435px; height: 70px;" />  </p><p>(b) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/dee503da-079f-4a7c-9d8e-10a81ff581ce-image.png" style="width: 416px; height: 130px;" /> </p><p>(c) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/adeac478-9f71-4fd1-a17c-61d94a4aa792-image.png" style="width: 492px; height: 120px;" />  </p><p>The boiling points of isomeric halo alkanes decrease with increase in branching.</p><p> </p>

### Q81. JEE Main 2020 (03 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The decreasing order of reactivity of the following compounds towards nucleophilic substitution (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math>) is:</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e55c559b-1e96-4ccc-b91a-ed1ebda23518-image.png" style="width: 248px; height: 269px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** (II) &gt; (III) &gt; (I) &gt; (IV)
- **(B)** (II) &gt; (III) &gt; (IV) &gt; (I)
- **(C)** (III) &gt; (II) &gt; (IV) &gt; (I)
- **(D)** (IV) &gt; (II) &gt; (III) &gt; (I)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) (II) &gt; (III) &gt; (IV) &gt; (I)**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Rate</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>reation</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Electrophilicity</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>alkyl</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>carbon</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>Steric</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>crowding</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>on</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> reaction depend upon <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math>, effect.<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> group increases the rate of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> reaction.</p>

</details>

<p>The mechanism of Sn1 reaction is given as:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>&#10230;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>&#8853;</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8214;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>&#8854;</mo></msup><mover><mo>&#10230;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>&#8854;</mo></msup></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>&#8854;</mo></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>lon</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>pair</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Solvent</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Speparated</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ion</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>pair</mi></math></p><p>A student writes general characteristics based on the given mechanism as :
(a) The reaction is favoured by weak nucleophiles.
(b) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>&#8853;</mo></msup></math> would be easily formed if the substituents are bulky <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math>
(c) The reaction is accompanied by recemization
(d) The reaction is favoured by non-polar solvents.</p><p>Which observations are correct?</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** (a) and (b)
- **(B)** (a) and (c)
- **(C)** (a), (b) and (c)
- **(D)** (b) and (d)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) (a), (b) and (c)**

**Detailed Solution:**

Above reaction is<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>1</mn></msup></msub></math> reaction as it proceed via formation of carbocation. Polar protic solvent is more suitable for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>1</mn></msup></msub></math> and recemisation takes place.

</details>

### Q83. JEE Main 2020 (02 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>Consider the reaction sequence given below :</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4351cc67-0a5c-4608-84fd-6e01a3218cc7-image.png" style="width: 410px; height: 183px;" /></p><p>Which of the following statements is true:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Changing the base from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>OH</mi><mo>&#8854;</mo></msup></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mmultiscripts><mi>OR</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mo>&#8854;</mo></mmultiscripts></math> will have no effect on reaction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(B)** Changing the concentration of base will have no effect on reaction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** Doubling the concentration of base will double the rate of both the reactions
- **(D)** Changing the concentration of base will have no effect on reaction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Changing the concentration of base will have no effect on reaction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>First reaction is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>SN</mi><mn>1</mn></msup></math> in which rate does not depend on conc. of nucleophile.</p><p>Second reaction is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>2</mn></math> reaction in which rate depends on conc. of base.</p>

</details>

### Q84. JEE Main 2020 (02 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The major product obtained from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>-elimination of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>bromo</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>fluoropentane</mi></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6abbcbce-70e1-4448-862f-5aff570a098a-image.png" style="width: 217px; height: 66px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1a78c8a0-700d-46a3-89b8-2360a4f18afa-image.png" style="width: 166px; height: 67px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9ccc381b-d772-4a5e-8c8d-be28910e697e-image.png" style="width: 227px; height: 67px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/37231c2c-b2c8-43a3-9373-3d105ffbe83d-image.png" style="width: 233px; height: 64px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1a78c8a0-700d-46a3-89b8-2360a4f18afa-image.png" style="width: 166px; height: 67px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Electronegativity of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> is higher than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Br</mi></math> so hydrogen near to fluorine is more acidic hence it will leave in presence of base.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/89b7059f-0b52-4cc1-91b6-72022208d04a-image.png" style="width: 456px; height: 142px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q85. JEE Main 2020 (02 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following compounds will show retention in configuration on nucleophile substitution by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>OH</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mi>ion</mi></math> ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/743f03c3-d81c-4f11-b712-38ea54ae7c9f-image.png" style="width: 134px; height: 121px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1c26dc52-108c-4713-a2ca-ffb2ff5fa2af-image.png" style="width: 157px; height: 77px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/17db19b8-8de8-457b-b415-85b76384ebd8-image.png" style="width: 157px; height: 77px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/65c2673c-59b4-4a12-b2b2-eda63197d9a4-image.png" style="width: 201px; height: 79px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/65c2673c-59b4-4a12-b2b2-eda63197d9a4-image.png" style="width: 201px; height: 79px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Option 1,2 and 3 have chiral carbon in which inversion of configuration takes place during <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow></mrow></msubsup><mn>2</mn></math> reaction but 4<sup>th</sup> option has achiral carbon associated with halogen  group so it will show retention after substitution reaction.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/65c2673c-59b4-4a12-b2b2-eda63197d9a4-image.png" style="width: 201px; height: 79px;" /></p>

</details>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi>A</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi>D</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi>C</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi>D</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi>A</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi>D</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi>C</mi></math>

### Q88. JEE Main 2020 (07 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>In the following reaction sequence, structures of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> are, respectively</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/11b77e6c-d099-4d41-b63c-fbec4d173e73.jpeg" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8ce7b1c9-f1e2-43ee-b8c4-1eae2275219c.jpeg">
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ddfe295e-2865-46e3-a42b-e6f34ac644fc.jpeg">
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3af97915-004c-46ba-883d-155a9be874d6.jpeg">
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/198169e7-332d-4806-9154-b5cb63d05b99.jpeg">

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3af97915-004c-46ba-883d-155a9be874d6.jpeg">**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Initially electrophilic addition reaction takes place and form alcohol and alkyl bromide. Then Wurtz reaction takes place.</p><p>The Wurtz reaction is a very useful reaction in the fields of organic chemistry and organometallic chemistry for the formation of alkanes. In this reaction, two different alkyl halides are coupled to yield a longer alkane chain with the help of sodium and dry ether solution.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a022e6b2-1d31-4625-8cb1-2707a2e55705.jpeg" /><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4489bb56-95ea-4c15-8d34-d8ebbabea576.jpeg" /></p>

</details>

### Q89. JEE Main 2020 (07 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the following reactions:

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d92198e7-a097-4b16-8aa9-0f6480e8a01a.jpeg" />

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/30f6c845-7f24-4d16-bea0-8b4fe122bf64.jpeg" />

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c1187b60-9c3f-49e7-aa85-1a3b6b0fdc0f.jpeg" />

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c805718f-3364-40fa-a294-85d48edfcdd3.jpeg" />

Which of these reactions are possible?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

### Q90. JEE Main 2020 (07 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

For the following reactions

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8f505117-3611-48dc-9add-d23cea38bbc3-image.png" style="width: 460px; height: 147px;" />

where,

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7c94c829-f5f2-42c7-9e2e-a15e9050bdf3-image.png" style="width: 334px; height: 92px;" />
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></msub></math> , are respectively, the rate constants for substitution and elimination, and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></msub></mfrac></math> , the correct option is ________

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></msub></math> <em>and</em> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></msub></math> <em>and</em> <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></msub></math> <em>and</em> <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></msub></math> <em>and</em> <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></msub></math> <em>and</em> <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c68e1dd3-1d53-4699-82e5-e02ebb2e9484-image.png" style="width: 457px; height: 212px;" />

(B) with more steric crowding forms elimination product compared to substitution

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>K</mtext> <mtext>e</mtext> </msub> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mtext>B</mtext><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><msub> <mtext>K</mtext> <mtext>e</mtext> </msub> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mtext>A</mtext><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><msub> <mi>&#956;</mi> <mtext>B</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>K</mtext> <mtext>s</mtext> </msub> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mtext>B</mtext><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>K</mtext> <mtext>e</mtext> </msub> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mtext>A</mtext><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow> </mfrac> <mo>&#60;</mo><msub> <mi>&#956;</mi> <mtext>A</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>K</mtext> <mtext>s</mtext> </msub> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mtext>A</mtext><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>K</mtext> <mtext>e</mtext> </msub> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mtext>A</mtext><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q91. JEE Main 2019 (12 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

An &lsquo;Assertion&rsquo; and a &lsquo;Reason&rsquo; is given below. Choose the correct answer from the following options:
Assertion <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>: Vinyl halides do not undergo nucleophilic substitution easily.
Reason <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>: Even though the intermediate carbocation is stabilized by loosely held <math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>-</mo></math> electrons, the cleavage is difficult because of the strong bonding.

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> are correct statements but <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is not the correct explanation of <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math>
- **(B)** Both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>)</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>)</mo></math> are wrong statements.
- **(C)** Both <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> are wrong statements and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>)</mo></math> is the correct explanation of <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is a correct statement but <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is a wrong statement.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is a correct statement but <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is a wrong statement.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><msub><mrow><mtext>CH</mtext></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></math> does not undergo unimolecular nucleophilic substitution <math><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo></math> reaction due to the formation of highly unstable cation <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mover><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>&#8853;</mo></mrow></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo></math> which can not be delocalized by <math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>-</mo></math> electrons. It does not give <math><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> because <math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> cloud is exposed, so, hindrance is there.

</details>

- **(B)** <math><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The rate of <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow></msub><mn>1</mn></math> reaction <math><mo>&#8733;</mo></math> stability of carbocation
stability of carbocation <math><mo>&#8733;</mo><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math> charge delocation.
<math><mo>&#8733;</mo><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8200;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8200;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math>
Order of effect effectiveness
<math><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>(</mo><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
D <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/10eb0bdc-0f07-4274-b7ae-486277a9fafb-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 172px;" /></p><p>C <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/28260e42-48e8-45ff-891e-ca39741cb142-image.png" style="width: 202px; height: 145px;" /></p><p>A <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/92c6998e-9992-4e24-a14d-c3105057d88a-image.png" style="width: 164px; height: 127px;" /></p><p>B <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/338eb1f3-fafb-4465-a39e-9046280181c4-image.png" style="width: 206px; height: 115px;" />
So, order of stability <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math></p>

</details>

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>.</mo></math> diagram for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>SN</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>SN</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> is two step reaction where in step <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> formation of carbocation is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>RDS</mi></math>.

</details>

### Q96. JEE Main 2019 (09 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Increasing order of reactivity of the following compounds for <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow></msub><mn>1</mn></math> substitution is:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/fc472485-6006-4ad7-9db4-13ee42472845-image.png" style="width: 450px; height: 272px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext>SN</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub></math> reactivity order <math><mo>&#8733;</mo></math> stability of carbocation
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/23fc0744-58b8-424d-9d82-eaadc833e6d7-image.png" style="width: 600px; height: 204px;" /></p><p>SN1 is unimolecular because the slow step of the reaction involves only the substrate, the reaction is unimolecular. Because only the substrate is present in the transition state, the rate of the reaction depends only on its concentration and not on the concentration of the nucleophile.</p>

</details>

### Q104. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>In the reaction given below what will be the major product at the end :

<img height="66" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4e135e13-f244-4513-b24e-0f88d6707fc9-image.png" width="213" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><img height="103" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f50acf26-f7b2-4320-9525-a466c38da08b-image.png" width="154" /></p>
- **(B)** <p><img height="99" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/cbd3d403-76d9-4483-a1e0-066352d1c001-image.png" width="116" /></p>
- **(C)** <p><img height="93" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d38776c6-ade5-4ad4-96cb-bb4ee60b6cc9-image.png" width="142" /></p>
- **(D)** <p><img height="95" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/66fd3b2a-7b7e-494f-9d51-186a67d1885c-image.png" width="135" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <p><img height="95" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/66fd3b2a-7b7e-494f-9d51-186a67d1885c-image.png" width="135" /></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The first step is anti addition of <math><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> across the double bond.

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7a45c302-0d57-4092-b3f1-10134ea6095f-qweaa.png" style="width: 372px; height: 115px;" />

In the second step, <math><mi>E</mi><mi>t</mi><msup><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> <math><mo>(</mo><mi>n</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> ) gets added by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> mechanism. And <math><mi>B</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> from the dashed position acts as the leaving group leads to the formation of a stable benzylic carbocation.

<img height="107" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1cc0e7fd-0196-435c-8de4-0b978ced6064-image.png" width="349" /></p>

</details>

### Q106. JEE Main 2018 (16 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The major product of the following reaction is:

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a27b0a4b-5bd4-4430-8e01-006164640afd-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 106px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/36742f5d-49d6-4416-8d0e-1fc16b3626f1-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 101px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9aaa4ba4-285f-45e4-b28c-5ed9add445e6-50ba25fc-cac9-4b0d-b137-3347dcece315-image.png" style="width: 187px; height: 119px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7989e5a7-1476-46db-8c19-5ad5d56b5dbf-image.png" style="width: 130px; height: 102px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/13149fae-21a8-475b-9deb-8644a9405d69-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 130px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9aaa4ba4-285f-45e4-b28c-5ed9add445e6-50ba25fc-cac9-4b0d-b137-3347dcece315-image.png" style="width: 187px; height: 119px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p style="text-align: justify;">This is hydrohalogenation reaction of alkene. This is an electrophilic addition reaction. Addition occurs via Markovnikov&#39;s rule. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HBr</mi></math> adds to the double bond of the styrene forming 1-bromo-1-phenylethane.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e60f2a4f-88b1-4998-99c5-434034b90414-image.png" style="width: 450px; height: 117px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q107. JEE Main 2018 (15 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Which of the following will most readily give the dehydrohalogenation product?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/531c8f1a-89e8-4da7-bbea-eca000e1a8f5-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 83px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6b64123e-016a-4221-b56f-3d5f38772fe0-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 107px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/68bac18a-2d37-4399-bbb5-e9ef0538caf7-image.png" style="width: 145px; height: 108px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/cf131d28-7e50-4e96-96a2-b37fefc99571-image.png" style="width: 145px; height: 113px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

### Q111. JEE Main 2017 (02 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following, upon treatment with tert-<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>BuONa</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math> followed by addition of bromine water, fails to decolourize the colour of bromine?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5923297e-1d76-442b-9e06-44f7c6d8df48-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 114px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/cba3bb67-9c1e-488c-b1e2-37cfa448bdff-image.png" style="width: 170px; height: 82px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d69ae38d-8ed2-4c72-9ed7-babd4ae5922d-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 93px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/800860e7-f308-4737-ad24-9b21dd57e027-image.png" style="width: 130px; height: 84px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/800860e7-f308-4737-ad24-9b21dd57e027-image.png" style="width: 130px; height: 84px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In the options, they have acidic <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#946;</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math> so an elimination reaction takes place and alkene will be formed.</p><p>And the formed alkene decolourizes the bromine water {<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#8853;</mo></mover></math> addition reaction of an alkene by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HOBr</mi></math>} while in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mn>4</mn></mfenced></math> it does not have <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#946;</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math> so, there is no formation of alkene, hence does not decolourize <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Br</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> water.</p>

</details>
