### Q112. JEE Main 2017 (02 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<math><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></math>Methyl<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo></math>pent<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></math>ene on reaction with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HBr</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math>in presence of peroxide forms an addition product. The number of possible stereoisomers for the product is

**Options:**

- **(A)** zero
- **(B)** two
- **(C)** four
- **(D)** six

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) four**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/abtsxaqdlccthprihqvnmxoompxpbsxqczzmyqkolhuttbkkaz.jpeg" />
In the presence of peroxide anti<span style="color: rgb(0, 0, 0); font-family: Arial, sans-serif;">-Markownikoff&#39;s product form.</span>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#10753;</mo></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math> attacks <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo></math>where hindrance is less.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> chiral centres are present in the product.

Number of stereoisomers <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msup><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></msup></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math>.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q113. JEE Main 2017 (02 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The increasing order of the reactivity of the following halides for the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> reaction is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/EM0178360.jpg" style="width: 422px; height: 87px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Rate of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> reaction is directly dependent on the stability of carbocation intermediate.
(II) is primary alkyl halide.
(I) is secondary alkyl halide.
Primary alkyl halide is less reactive towards <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> reaction than secondary alkyl halide.
In (III), the carbocation generated is stabilised due to resonance. Hence, (III) is most reactive towards <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></math> reaction.</p>

</details>

---

### Q114. JEE Main 2017 (02 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The major product obtained in the following reaction is,

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bde6b1fa-4245-4195-9256-32b45e65afad-05-03-22.png" style="width: 308px; height: 111px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>CH</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>CHC</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mo>+</mo></mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>CH</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>OtBu</mi></mfenced><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mo>-</mo></mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>CH</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>OtBu</mi></mfenced><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mo>&#177;</mo></mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>CH</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>OtBu</mi></mfenced><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>CH</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>CHC</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Tert-butyl oxide is a strong base and a weak nucleophile.</p><p>In presence of a strong base and heat, elimination reaction favours over substitution reaction.</p><p>The type of elimination taking place here is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#946;</mi></math>-elimination</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/80b8a7f5-f62d-4586-8b53-817fb298a19b-o5-03-222.png" style="width: 411px; height: 118px;" />So, option A is the correct answer.</p><p> </p>

</details>

Bromination of cyclohexene under conditions given below yields:

### Q116. JEE Main 2016 (10 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which one of the following reagents is not<strong> </strong>suitable for the elimination reaction?

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/image3-70fcda5d-32da-48b9-b599-5e4090177024.jpg" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>/</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>To get the desired product, an elimination reaction needs to occur.</p><p>For an elimination reaction, a strong base <math> <mrow> <mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mover> <mtext>O</mtext> <mo>&#8722;</mo> </mover> <mtext>Et,</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover> <mtext>O</mtext> <mo>&#8722;</mo> </mover> <mtext>H</mtext></mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow> </math> needed. Because <math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>I</mtext> <mo>&#8722;</mo> </msup> </mrow> </math> is not basic, <math> <mrow> <mtext>NaI</mtext></mrow> </math> is not suitable for the following reaction.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/image4-286c3b48-ac71-4ca3-a7d5-8f162b000902.jpg" />

With <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I,</mi></math> substitution takes place.</p>

</details>

### Q117. JEE Main 2016 (03 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>- chloro - <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> - methylpentane on reaction with sodium methoxide in methanol yields:

(i) <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/image26-9f76e861-1a2c-4ae1-a78a-0c87afd0ee14.png" style="height: 145px; width: 200px;" />

(ii) <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/image27-288ab881-f90e-49d1-8615-d2039db73687.png" style="height: 81px; width: 200px;" />

(iii) <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/image28-31e7583f-5ca6-4813-a89b-4933cb7c6b94.png" style="height: 79px; width: 200px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** iii only
- **(B)** i and ii
- **(C)** i and iii
- **(D)** All of these

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) All of these**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>When given reactant reacts with the sodium methoxide solution then both substitution and elimination reactions are possible.</p><p>Substitution reaction gives the following product through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>1</mn></msup></msub></math> reaction.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>1</mn></msup></math> will give the following products<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/image29-38fbee92-8e46-467c-a284-85200932d56e.jpg" />


<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/135071.png" style="width: 674px; height: 200px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q119. JEE Main 2015 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>What is the major product expected from the following reaction?

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0d105fc9-c3bc-4646-bd1c-39991febc053-image.png" style="width: 166px; height: 110px;" />

Where D is an isotope of hydrogen.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0fbee21a-9e5c-4fc4-8266-5b3d9e61692b-image.png" style="width: 139px; height: 150px;" /></p>
- **(B)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6defc4ff-f65d-4b67-bbe0-eb4db2ef6689-image.png" style="width: 139px; height: 150px;" /></p>
- **(C)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/eaa56f9f-fe3f-4c7a-b8b5-a6e199a61582-image.png" style="width: 141px; height: 150px;" /></p>
- **(D)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/469af007-92fc-44fc-881c-d362a7af1998-image.png" style="width: 140px; height: 150px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0fbee21a-9e5c-4fc4-8266-5b3d9e61692b-image.png" style="width: 139px; height: 150px;" /></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The hydrohalogenation of alkenes involves breaking a carbon to carbon double bond, followed by the electrophilic addition of a hydrogen atom and halogen. The halide will add to the more substituted carbon following Markovnikov&#39;s rule.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0d105fc9-c3bc-4646-bd1c-39991febc053-image.png" style="width: 166px; height: 110px;" /><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0fbee21a-9e5c-4fc4-8266-5b3d9e61692b-image.png" style="width: 139px; height: 150px;" />

anti addition reaction.</p>

</details>

### Q120. JEE Main 2015 (04 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The synthesis of alkyl fluorides is best accomplished by:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Swarts reaction
- **(B)** Free radical fluorination
- **(C)** Sandmeyer's  reaction
- **(D)** Finkelstein reaction

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Swarts reaction**

**Detailed Solution:**

Alkyl fluorides is best accomplished by swarts reaction i.e. heating an alkyl chloride/bromide in the presence of metallic fluoride such as AgF, Hg<sub>2</sub>F<sub>2</sub>, CoF<sub>2</sub>, SbF<sub>3</sub>.

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>CH</mtext> <mn>3</mn> </msub> <mtext>Br</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>AgF</mtext> <mo>→</mo> <msub> <mtext>CH</mtext> <mn>3</mn></msub><mtext>F</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>AgBr</mtext> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q121. JEE Main 2014 (19 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The major product formed when 1, 1, 1- trichloropropane is treated with aqueous potassium hydroxide is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2 - Propanol
- **(B)** Propyne
- **(C)** Propionic acid
- **(D)** 1 - Propanol

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Propionic acid**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p style="text-align: justify;">The major product formed when 1, 1, 1-trichloro-propane is treated with aqueous potassium hydroxide is propionic acid.</p><p style="text-align: justify;">Alc. KOH causes elimination in the molecule of haloalkane generally, while aq. KOH leads to substitution. Here aqueous KOH is used, substitution reaction is most preferable.</p><p style="text-align: justify;">The initial reaction is the nucleophilic substitution, which will form a trihydroxy compound, since, three hydroxy group on one carbon is unstable and result in dehydration and form carboxylic acid.</p><p style="text-align: justify;"><u><strong><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/EM0026260_AnsExp_1.jpg" style="width: 486px; height: 113px;" /><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/EM0026260_AnsExp_2.jpg" style="width: 494px; height: 145px;" /></strong></u></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q122. JEE Main 2014 (12 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The major product obtained in the photo catalysed bromination of 2-methylbutane is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1-bromo-2-methylbutane
- **(B)** 1-bromo-3-methylbutane
- **(C)** 2-bromo-3-methylbutane
- **(D)** 2-bromo-2-methylbutane

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 2-bromo-2-methylbutane**

**Detailed Solution:**

The order of substitution in different alkanes is
$$
3^{\circ}>2^{\circ}>1^{\circ}
$$
Thus the bromination of 2-methyl butane mainly gives 2-Bromo-2 - methyl butane
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/AlL06VgFamgm0nV54Nl5tltnUUl0DXeKD6d5PMn5Gmc.original.fullsize.png">

</details>

### Q123. JEE Main 2014 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

In a nucleophilic substitution reaction : <math> <mrow> <mtext>R</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>Br</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mtext>Cl</mtext> <mo>-</mo> </msup> <mover> <mo>&#10230;</mo> <mtext>DMF</mtext> </mover> <mtext>R</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>Cl</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mtext>Br</mtext> <mo>-</mo> </msup> </mrow> </math> ,  which one of the following undergoes complete inversion of configuration ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** C<sub>6</sub>H<sub>5</sub>CHC<sub>6</sub>H<sub>5</sub>Br
- **(B)** C<sub>6</sub>H<sub>5</sub>CCH<sub>3</sub>C<sub>6</sub>H<sub>5</sub>Br
- **(C)** C<sub>6</sub>H<sub>5</sub>CH<sub>2</sub>Br
- **(D)** C<sub>6</sub>H<sub>5</sub>CH<sub>2</sub>CH<sub>2</sub>Br

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) C<sub>6</sub>H<sub>5</sub>CH<sub>2</sub>CH<sub>2</sub>Br**

**Detailed Solution:**

In <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> (nucleophilic bimolecular substitution) inversion takes place

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mtext>stearic&#160;hindrance</mtext></mfrac></math>

Rate of R&ndash;X is 1&deg; &gt; 2&deg; &gt; 3&deg;

</details>

### Q124. JEE Main 2014 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

For the compounds CH<sub>3</sub>Cl, CH<sub>3</sub>Br, CH<sub>3</sub>I and CH<sub>3</sub>F, the correct order of increasing C-halogen bond length is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** CH<sub>3</sub>F &lt; CH<sub>3</sub>Br &lt; CH<sub>3</sub>Cl &lt; CH<sub>3</sub>I
- **(B)** CH<sub>3</sub>F &lt; CH<sub>3</sub>I &lt; CH<sub>3</sub>Br &lt; CH<sub>3</sub>Cl
- **(C)** CH<sub>​3</sub>F &lt; CH<sub>3</sub>Cl &lt; CH<sub>3</sub>Br &lt; CH<sub>3</sub>I
- **(D)** CH<sub>3</sub>Cl &lt; CH<sub>3</sub>Br &lt; CH<sub>3</sub>F &lt; CH<sub>3</sub>I

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) CH<sub>​3</sub>F &lt; CH<sub>3</sub>Cl &lt; CH<sub>3</sub>Br &lt; CH<sub>3</sub>I**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>C-halogen bond length totally depends on the electronegativity difference between Carbon and halogen. The more the difference and the less is the bond length. The electronegativity order of halogens is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Br</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Cl</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>. Hence, the correct order of increasing C-halogen bond length is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn><mi>Cl</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn><mi>Br</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Or we can say that as the size of halogen increases C-halogen bond length also increases.</p>

</details>

### Q125. JEE Main 2014 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Allyl phenyl ether can be prepared by heating :

**Options:**

- **(A)** CH<sub>2</sub> = CH - Br + C<sub>6</sub>H<sub>5</sub> - CH<sub>2</sub> - ONa
- **(B)** C<sub>6</sub>H<sub>5</sub> - CH = CH - Br + CH<sub>3</sub> - ONa
- **(C)** CH<sub>2</sub><sub> </sub>=​ CH - CH<sub>2</sub> - Br + C<sub>6</sub>H<sub>5</sub>ONa
- **(D)** C<sub>6</sub>H<sub>5</sub>Br + CH<sub>2</sub> = CH - CH<sub>2</sub> - ONa

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) CH<sub>2</sub><sub> </sub>=​ CH - CH<sub>2</sub> - Br + C<sub>6</sub>H<sub>5</sub>ONa**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>It is a Williamson&#39;s ether synthesis reaction. It is a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> reaction. Aryl halides will not undergo nucleophilic substitution reactions readily. Hence, allyl-phenyl ether can be prepared by reaction of allyl halide and phenoxide ion as follows</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9d81acde-820c-4941-bd2e-fdfcbcf50301-image.png" style="width: 392px; height: 86px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q126. JEE Main 2014 (06 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

In S<sub>N</sub>2 reactions, the correct order of reactivity for the following compounds :
CH<sub>3</sub>Cl, CH<sub>3</sub>CH<sub>2</sub>Cl, (CH<sub>3</sub>)<sub>2</sub>CHCl and (CH<sub>3</sub>)<sub>3</sub>CCl is :

- **(A)** CH<sub>3</sub>Cl &gt; (CH<sub>3</sub>)<sub>2</sub>CHCl &gt; CH<sub>3</sub>CH<sub>2</sub>Cl &gt; (CH<sub>3</sub>)<sub>3</sub>CCl
- **(B)** CH<sub>3</sub>Cl &gt; CH<sub>3</sub>CH<sub>2</sub>Cl &gt; (CH<sub>3</sub>)<sub>2</sub>CHCl &gt; (CH<sub>3</sub>)<sub>3</sub>CCl
- **(C)** CH<sub>3</sub>CH<sub>2</sub>Cl &gt; CH<sub>3</sub>Cl &gt; (CH<sub>3</sub>)<sub>2</sub>CHCl &gt; (CH<sub>3</sub>)<sub>3</sub>CCl
- **(D)** (CH<sub>3</sub>)<sub>2</sub>CHCl &gt; CH<sub>3</sub>CH<sub>2</sub>Cl &gt; CH<sub>3</sub>Cl &gt; (CH<sub>3</sub>)<sub>3</sub>CCl

<p style="text-align: justify;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext>S</mtext><mtext>N</mtext></msub><mtext>2</mtext></math> involves the formation of a pentavalent intermediate, and the reactivity depends on steric hindrance. ### Q127. JEE Main 2014 (06 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The correct statement for the molecule <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>CsI</mtext> <mn>3</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>, is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** It is a covalent molecule.
- **(B)** It contains <math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>Cs</mtext> <mo>+</mo> </msup> </mrow> </math> and <math> <mrow> <msubsup> <mtext>I</mtext> <mn>3</mn> <mo>-</mo> </msubsup> </mrow> </math> ions.
- **(C)** It contains <math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>Cs</mtext> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mo>+</mo> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math> and <math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>I</mtext> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math> ions.
- **(D)** It contains <math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>Cs</mtext> <mo>+</mo> </msup> <msup> <mo>, I</mo> <mo>-</mo> </msup> </mrow> </math> and lattice <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>I</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math> molecule.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>I</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mtext>I</mtext> <mo>-</mo> </msup> <mo>→</mo> <msubsup> <mtext>I</mtext> <mn>3</mn> <mo>-</mo> </msubsup> </mrow> </math>

Cs will always be in +1 oxidation state.

</details>

### Q128. JEE Main 2013 (25 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

In nucleophilic substitution reaction, order of halogens as incoming (attacking) nucleophile is: 
$\mathrm{I}^{-}>\mathrm{Br}^{-}>\mathrm{Cl}^{-}$
The order of halogens as departing nucleophile should be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Br}^{-}>\mathrm{I}^{-}>\mathrm{Cl}^{-}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{I}^{-}>\mathrm{Br}^{-}>\mathrm{Cl}^{-}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Cl}^{-}>\mathrm{Br}^{-}>\mathrm{I}^{-}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Cl}^{-}>\mathrm{I}^{-}>\mathrm{Br}^{-}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{I}^{-}>\mathrm{Br}^{-}>\mathrm{Cl}^{-}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since the leaving group breaks away as a base, it is easier to displace weaker bases as compared to stronger bases. Thus less basic the substituent, the more easily it is displaced.
Since the basic strength of the given groups is in order.
$\mathrm{I}^{-} < \mathrm{Br}^{-} < \mathrm{Cl}^{-}$
Thus the order of halogen leaving groups is
$\mathrm{I}^{-}>\mathrm{Br}^{-}>\mathrm{Cl}^{-}$

</details>

---

### Q129. JEE Main 2013 (23 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The order of reactivity of the given haloalkanes towards nucleophile is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{RI}>\mathrm{RBr}>\mathrm{RCl}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{RCl}>\mathrm{RBr}>\mathrm{RI}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{RBr}>\mathrm{RCl}>\mathrm{RI}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{RBr}>\mathrm{RI}>\mathrm{RCl}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{RI}>\mathrm{RBr}>\mathrm{RCl}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a given alkyl group, the order of reactivity is
$\frac{\mathrm{R}-\mathrm{I}>\mathrm{R}-\mathrm{Br}>\mathrm{R}-\mathrm{Cl}>\mathrm{R}-\mathrm{F}}{\text { increasing bond energy }}$
decreasing halogen reactivity.
This order depends on the carbon-halogen bond energy; the carbon-fluorine bond energy is maximum and thus fluorides are least reactive while carbon-iodine bond energy is minimum hence iodides are most reactive.

</details>

### Q135. JEE Main 2012 (Offline)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

How many chiral compounds are possible on monochlorination of $2-$methyl butane ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $8$
- **(B)** $2$
- **(C)** $4$
- **(D)** $6$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{H}_3 \mathrm{C}-\mathrm{CH}_2-\mathrm{CH}\left(\mathrm{CH}_3\right)-\mathrm{CH}_3$ on monochlorination gives
$\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{C}(\mathrm{Cl})-\mathrm{CH}_2-\mathrm{CH}\left(\mathrm{CH}_3\right)-\mathrm{CH}_3$
(I)
Achiral
$\mathrm{H}_3 \mathrm{C}-\mathrm{CH}(\mathrm{Cl})-\mathrm{CH}\left(\mathrm{CH}_3\right)-\mathrm{CH}_3$
(II)
Chiral
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/Lrx8ym_zFZqf92rd-Xa5TofLPcgoxWLI1mi981Zb1Fg.original.fullsize.png">

(III)
Achiral<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/KTXjE5avk2BRbVr4-hOcLHi9kfhtHMV9_718H2Tn9E0.original.fullsize.png">

(IV)
chiral

</details>

### Q136. JEE Main 2010

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Consider the following bromides :
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/labcWmUGy05BucAllMFGm5URvjsuZWIKYQ7DrHA4Nbg.original.fullsize.png">

The correct order of $\mathrm{S}_{\mathrm{N}} 1$ reactivity is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $B>C>A$
- **(B)** $B>A>C$
- **(C)** $C>B>A$
- **(D)** $A>B>C$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $B>C>A$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{S}_{\mathrm{N}} 1$ proceeds via carbocation intermediate, the most stable one forming the product faster. Hence reactivity order for $\mathrm{A}, \mathrm{B}, \mathrm{C}$ depends on stability of carbocation created.
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b_7x78l58e8mpq6u5_deqWwqQCOsDj-uOEvfE32OjjY.original.fullsize.png">

</details>

### Q137. JEE Main 2008

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The organic chloro compound, which shows complete stereochemical inversion during a $\mathrm{S}_{\mathrm{N}} 2$ reaction, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left(\mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{H}_5\right)_2 \mathrm{CHCl}$
- **(B)** $\left(\mathrm{CH}_3\right)_3 \mathrm{CCl}$
- **(C)** $\left(\mathrm{CH}_3\right)_2 \mathrm{CHCl}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{Cl}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{Cl}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For $\mathrm{S}_{\mathrm{N}} 2$ reaction, the $\mathrm{C}$ atom is least hindered towards the attack of nucleophile in the case of $\left(\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{Cl}\right)$.
Hence, (D) is the correct answer.

</details>

---

### Q138. JEE Main 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Which of the following is the correct order of decreasing $\mathrm{SN}^2$ reactivity?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{RCH}_2 \mathrm{X}>\mathrm{R}_3 \mathrm{CX}>\mathrm{R}_2 \mathrm{CHX}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{RCH}_2 \mathrm{X}>\mathrm{R}_2 \mathrm{CHX}>\mathrm{R}_3 \mathrm{CX}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{R}_3 \mathrm{CX}>\mathrm{R}_2 \mathrm{CHX}>\mathrm{RCH}_2 X$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{R}_2 \mathrm{CHX}>\mathrm{R}_3 \mathrm{CX}>\mathrm{RCH}_2 \mathrm{X}$ 
$(\mathrm{X}=$ a halogen $)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{RCH}_2 \mathrm{X}>\mathrm{R}_2 \mathrm{CHX}>\mathrm{R}_3 \mathrm{CX}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

More is the steric hindrance at the carbon bearing the halogen, lesser is the $\mathrm{S}_{\mathrm{N}} 2$ reactivity. 
Hence, (B) is correct.

</details>
